Figure 16
.
The replication program written in
P4R-Type
.

When a programmer uses our
P4R-Type
API, the Scala compiler
spots several possible errors that may occur when updating multiple
switches with different P4 configurations:

•

Using non-existent table or action names (e.g., due to typos)

•

Inserting the wrong type of entries in a table (e.g., wrong number of match
fields)

•

Using an existing action in an existing table that does not support it (e.g., an entry in
firewall
referencing
ipv4_forward
)

•

Passing the wrong type of arguments to an action (e.g., an entry in
ipv4_lpm
referencing action
ipv4_forward
,
but passing only one argument)

Generated Types in Scala 3.

Using the types generated by the tool, the replication program written in

P4R-Type
is shown in
Figure

16
. Note that the API
interface is relatively minimal and similar to the Python API. For instance,
compare the
insert
call in line 9–11 to the Python code in
Figure

1
.
The difference here is that an error like the one shown in
Figure

1

would be caught at compile time by the Scala 3 type system. For example, using
"
Process.forward_packet
"
instead of
"
Process.drop
"

on line 11 would yield a type error:
“a value of type
s.TA[("Process.firewall")]

is required”
.

On lines 1-4, the connection to each switch is established. Note that the

connect
methods are specific to each configuration, unlike the other
P4Runtime operations which are part of a generic package:
connect
returns
an
FP4Channel
instance with predefined type parameters, which in turn
constrain the
read
/
insert
/
modify
/
delete

operations that can be performed on that channel.
Consider e.g. lines 9–11 in
Figure

16
: in the
insert

call, the
tableEntry
parameter is constrained to only accept table entries
that satisfy the switch configuration of channel
s
. Since
s

ranges over a list of channels having two different types of switches
(
config1
and
config2
), such entries must be valid in
both
switch
configurations. Since both configurations share a
"
Process.firewall
"

table, the program compiles. Instead, if an otherwise valid entry for e.g. the

"
Process.ipv4_lpm
"
table is provided, the code would not compile,
as that table is defined in
config1
but not in
config2
.

8.2.
Port Forwarding Management

We implemented a control plane program for
port forwarding
, which is a
Network Address Translations (NAT) service typically offered e.g. by Internet
routers. We use the same topology as in
Figure

14
, but we
assume that N1, N2, and N3 are local networks, while N4 is an external network.
The goal is to allow external clients to connect to servers hosted in the
internal networks. To this end, S4 applies a set of NAT rules saying e.g. that:

•

each packet received on the external S4 interface, and having
destination IP address
1.2.3.4
and port
42
, should be translated
to have destination IP address
10.1.0.4
and port 1042
(and vice versa for the internal S4 interface).

We developed a program (using
P4R-Type
) that offers a command line interface to
connect to S4 and query, add, and delete its NAT rules. The program reads and
modifies two P4 tables called
nat_ingress
and
nat_egress

containing the translations for incoming and outgoing packets.
Translated packets are then forwarded according to the entries of a table called

ipv4_forward
(similar to the one used in

Section

8.1
).

8.3.
Load Balancing

We implemented a control plane program for load balancing packet transfers. We
use the same topology as in
Figure

14
, and the goal is
for S1 to equally destribute all packets bound for N4 between its outgoing
ports to S2, S3 and S4.
To implement this, we use a P4 entity called
counter
,
8
8

8

Counters are not modelled in
F_{\text{P4R}}
; they can be
easily added e.g. as a new case to the union type of
P4Entity

(
Figure

4
).
which can be incremented by the data plane and read by the control plane. We
configure the data plane of S1 with one counter per output port, and rules that
increment a counter every time a packet is forwarded through the corresponding
port. Our control plane program then periodically reads the counter values (using
the
P4R-Type
method
readCounter
, similar to
read
for P4
tables)
and updates the packet forwarding rules (using the
P4R-Type
method
modify
).

8.4.
On the Role of Match Types and Singleton Types

We now discuss whether our results could be achieved with a different design
that, while still satisfying requirements
(R1)
,

(R2)
, and
(R3)
in

Section

1
, would not rely on match types nor singleton types, and would
be therefore less tied to the Scala 3 programming language. Let us consider the
case study in
Section

8.1
, and how we could address
it in a subset of Scala 3
without
match nor singleton types.

To ensure that the table entries described in a P4Info file are constructed
correctly, we would need to generate a dedicated data type for each table, with
argument types capturing the constraints on actions and parameters. We would also
need to constrain channel types to valid table entry types, to ensure that

read
/
insert
/
modify
/
delete
only use table
entries of the correct type. E.g. in the case of the first P4Info metadata in

Figure

15
we might generate a set of type definitions
like:

⬇

1

package

config1

2

3

case

class

ActionWildcard
()

4

case

class

ActionDrop
()

5

case

class

ActionForwardPacket
(
addr
:

ByteString
,

port
:

ByteString
)

6

7

type

FirewallAction

=

ActionDrop

|

ActionWildcard

8

case

class

FirewallTableEntry
(
fields
:

Option
[
FP4_LPM
],

action
:

FirewallAction
)

9

10

type

IPV4Action

=

ActionDrop

|

ActionForwardPacket

|

ActionWildcard

11

case

class

IPV4TableEntry
(
fields
:

(
FP4_Exact
,

Option
[
FP4_LPM
]),

action
:

IPV4Action
)

12

13

def

connect
(...):

FP4Channel
[
FirewallTableEntry

|

IPV4TableEntry
]

=

...

A program written with the resulting API would look like:

⬇

1

val

s1

=

config1
.
connect
(0,

"127.0.0.1"
,

50051)

2

insert
(
s1
,

config1
.
FirewallTableEntry
(
Some
(
FP4_LPM
(...)),

config1
.
ActionDrop
))

The type definitions outlined above are roughly as compact as the match types we
generate.
9
9

9

These definitions may be more verbose in languages without the type union
“
|
”, going against requirement
(R3)
. E.g. in
F# or OCaml,
FirewallAction
and
IPV4Action
would be rendered
as labelled sum types, and each action used in more than one table would result
in duplicated label definition (in this example, this would apply to

ActionDrop
and
ActionWildcard
).

However, the main drawback of such type definitions
is that they are substantially more laborious to formalise: we would need to
extend the typing system of
F_{\text{P4R}}
(
Definition

4.2
) with a
nominal environment to collect type definitions, and the formal encoding from
P4Info metadata to types would be significantly more complex than our

Definition

5.2
. As a consequence, stating and proving
results like our
Theorems

6.1
and
6.4
would be
considerably harder, hampering requirement
(R1)
.

On the practical side, another drawback of the type definitions outlined above
is that they would make the API more cumbersome and limited:
e.g. it would be hard or
impossible to write code like lines 7–11 in
Figure

16
, where
the
insert
operation works on channels with different P4 configurations

config1
and
config2
. The reason is that channels
s1

and
s2
would only support table entries of type

config1.FirewallTableEntry
, whereas channels
s3
and

s4
would only support
config2.FirewallTableEntry
: such types
would be unrelated and could not be unified, hence a programmer would need to
duplicate the code of the
insert
operations. One might try to mitigate
this duplication by leveraging structural typing (available e.g. in TypeScript,
or in OCaml
struct
s) — but then, the signature of the API method

insert
would become non-trivial and the feasibility of this approach
would require further research. Instead, the match types produced by our
encoding in
Definition

5.2
allow the Scala compiler to verify
that the table entries for
"Process.firewall"
have the same type under
both
config1
and
config2
, hence the code in

Figure

16
type-checks.

9.
Related Work

The programmability of SDNs comes at the cost of
complexity and attack surfaces of modern networks
(
Kreutz et al. 2013
)
.
Several proposals address complementary problems
to our work by giving formal semantics to the data plane language
(
Doenges et al. 2021
;
Alshnakat et al. 2022
;
Peterson et al. 2023
)
and by developing
static
(
Liu et al. 2018
;
Stoenescu et al. 2018
;
Eichholz et al. 2022
)
and dynamic
(
Nötzli et al. 2018
;
Shukla et al. 2020
)
analysis tools for the data plane.

Several tools have been developed to verify various network
properties. Header Space Analysis
(
Kazemian et al. 2012
)
is a framework
that can analyse reachability and identify loops, among other properties, of dynamic networks. Both the data plane and the control plane must be represented in the abstract framework.
NetKAT
(
Anderson et al. 2014
)
, and the more recent DyNetKat
(
Caltais et al. 2022
)
, provides a network language
which encompasses both data plane and control plane,
with formal semantics and develops syntactic techniques for proving network reachability, non-interference, and correctness of program transformation.
Batfish
(
Fogel et al. 2015
)
uses a declarative approach to define network behavior via logical relations that represent both data and control plane. The framework allows to check if any reachable network configurations (and packets) can violate forwarding properties.

The main difference with our proposal is that these models are
non-executable specifications and are much more abstract than the languages used to program SDNs.
Therefore, they do not directly provide a method to program the
control plane. Verifying actual control software using these models requires to map software behavior to these specifications, which is extremely hard when the control plane is developed using a general-purpose language like Python or Scala.
Moreover, many of these models
assume a configurable, but not programmable,
data plane, which supports a limited and predefined
set of protocols (e.g., SDNs using OpenFlow
(
McKeown et al. 2008
)
).
Instead, our proposal provides a programming framework
for the control plane that can interact with arbitrary P4 data planes,
and that can statically prevent invalid table manipulations.

10.
Conclusion and Future Work

We presented
P4R-Type
, a novel verified API for P4Runtime programs written in
Scala 3. As a foundation for
P4R-Type
, we presented the first formal model of
P4Runtime networks, where servers interact with client applications written in
the calculus
F_{\text{P4R}}
; we also developed a typing system for

F_{\text{P4R}}
(including match types and singleton types, inspired by Scala 3)
and proved that well-typed
F_{\text{P4R}}
clients interact correctly with the
surrounding servers (
Theorems

6.1
and
6.4
). These
correctness results are inherited by actual P4 control programs that use
our
P4R-Type
API.

This paper is a stepping stones toward a broader objective: a fully-verified P4
development pipeline encompassing the verification of
both
the control
plane and the data plane, ensuring that configuration updates applied by control
programs never compromise desired network properties. This objective determines our future work, outlined below.

While our type system is sound in the sense that well-typed programs never get
stuck, a server may still in some cases reject an update by producing a

false
response value (for
Insert
,
Modify
or
Delete
).
Not all these cases can be statically verified (e.g. trying to insert a table
entry that already exists in the server), but some cases may be prevented by
further typing constraints.
For example, instead of using the same
P4Entity
type for all of the
operations that handle table entries, we may adopt distinct formats or
restrictions on table entries for distinct operations — e.g. the
Insert

operation does not in general accept entries where
\mathit{table\_matches}=\texttt{"*"}
, but the
Read
operation always does. A solution to this could
be to generate a distinct set of match types for each operation: this should not
drastically change the formalization nor the proofs.
Network properties like reachability of a node,
enforcement of access control list,
and presence of loops,
for systems with programmable data plane cannot be verified by looking only at
the control plane.
In order to verify these properties,
we plan to extend our semantics with P4Runtime stream messages
and integrate it with existing semantics of P4.
We may also need to formalise more detailed P4Runtime server semantics, e.g. to
model P4 network elements that perform delayed table updates, have background
processes, or communicate with each other. We expect that, thanks to our
adoption of an early LTS semantics for clients and servers
(
Section

5.1
), we will be able to adapt the server
semantics, while reusing most of the current proofs and results involving

F_{\text{P4R}}
clients.

Acknowledgements.
This work was partially supported by the DTU Nordic Five Tech Alliance grant
“Safe and secure software-defined networks in P4” and the Sponsor Horizon Europe
https://research-and-innovation.ec.europa.eu/funding/funding-opportunities/funding-programmes-and-open-calls/horizon-europe_en

grant no. Grant #101093006 “TaRDIS.”

Term Substitution in Terms

\displaystyle v[x\mapsto t]\

\displaystyle=\ v

\displaystyle y[x\mapsto t]\

\displaystyle=\ t\quad(x=y)

\displaystyle y[x\mapsto t]\

\displaystyle=\ y\quad(x\neq y)

\displaystyle({t_{1}}\mathbin{\texttt{{:}\!{:}}}{t_{2}})[x\mapsto t]\

\displaystyle=\ {(t_{1}[x\mapsto t])}\mathbin{\texttt{{:}\!{:}}}{t_{2}[x\mapsto t]}

\displaystyle(\texttt{head }t_{l})[x\mapsto t]\

\displaystyle=\ \texttt{head }t_{l}[x\mapsto t]

\displaystyle(\texttt{tail }t_{l})[x\mapsto t]\

\displaystyle=\ \texttt{tail }t_{l}[x\mapsto t]

\displaystyle\{f_{1}=t_{1},...,f_{n}=t_{n}\}[x\mapsto t]\

\displaystyle=\ \{f_{1}=t_{1}[x\mapsto t],...,f_{n}=t_{n}[x\mapsto t]\}

\displaystyle(t_{r}.f)[x\mapsto t]\

\displaystyle=\ (t_{r}[x\mapsto t]).f

\displaystyle(\lambda y:T.\ t_{0})[x\mapsto t]\

\displaystyle=\ \lambda y:T.\ t_{0}[x\mapsto t]

\displaystyle(t_{1}\ t_{2})[x\mapsto t]\

\displaystyle=\ (t_{1}[x\mapsto t])\ (t_{2}[x\mapsto t])

\displaystyle(\char 12\relax{X}{T}t_{0})[x\mapsto t]\

\displaystyle=\ \char 12\relax{X}{T}t_{0}[x\mapsto t]

\displaystyle(t_{1}\ T_{2})[x\mapsto t]\

\displaystyle=\ (t_{1}[x\mapsto t])\ T_{2}

\displaystyle=\ \texttt{let }y\texttt{ = }(t_{0}[x\mapsto t])\texttt{ in }(t_{1}[x\mapsto t])

\displaystyle op(\overline{w})[x\mapsto t]\

\displaystyle=\ op(\overline{w[x\mapsto t]})

Appendix B
Proofs

This appendix section presents the proofs for type safety.
The proof strategy closely mirrors the approach in
Blanvillain et al. 2022
,
with the main deviations being the proof cases for new rules.

Lemma B.1 (Disjointness of values).

\Gamma\not\vdash\underline{v}<:T\Rightarrow\Gamma\vdash\underline{v}\diamond T

Proof.

The proof of the proposition is by contradiction.

Suppose that
{\Gamma\not\vdash\underline{v}<:T}
, but
{\exists T^{\prime}:\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}<:\underline{v}\wedge\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}<:T}
.

Since
\underline{v}
can only subtype to itself by
ST-Refl
, we have from the left conjunction that
T^{\prime}=\underline{v}
.

But then we can show from the right conjunction that
\Gamma\vdash\underline{v}<:T
, which is a contradiction.
∎

Lemma B.2 (Disjointness of values).

\Gamma\not\vdash\underline{v}<:T\Rightarrow\Gamma\vdash\underline{v}\diamond T

Proof.

The proof of the proposition is by contradiction.

Suppose that
{\Gamma\not\vdash\underline{v}<:T}
, but
{\exists T^{\prime}:\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}<:\underline{v}\wedge\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}<:T}
.

Since
\underline{v}
can only subtype to itself by
ST-Refl
, we have from the left conjunction that
T^{\prime}=\underline{v}
.

But then we can show from the right conjunction that
\Gamma\vdash\underline{v}<:T
, which is a contradiction.
∎

B.1.
Permutation

Lemma B.3 (Permutation).

If
\vdash\Gamma\ \text{env}
,
\vdash\Gamma^{\prime}\ \text{env}
, and
\Gamma^{\prime}
is a permutation of
\Gamma
, then

(1)

If
\Gamma\vdash T\ \text{type}
, then
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T\ \text{type}
.

(2)

If
\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}
, then
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T<:T^{\prime}
.

(3)

If
\Gamma\vdash t:T
, then
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t:T
.

Proof.

(1) and (2) are proven simultaneously by induction on the two derivations
\Gamma\vdash T\ \text{type}
and
\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}
.

(1)

By induction on the derivation of
\Gamma\vdash T\ \text{type}
.

•

Type-Int
,
Type-Bool
,
Type-Unit
,
Type-String
,
Type-Bytes

Immediate from using the same rule with
\vdash\Gamma^{\prime}\ \text{env}
in the premise.

•

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},X<:T,\Gamma_{2}

\Gamma^{\prime}
is a permutation of
\Gamma
, then there must be some
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}

such that
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},X<:T,\Gamma_{2}
.
The result then follows from
Type-TV

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typeJ{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T_1}}{T_2}\end{gathered}}{\typeJ{\Gamma}{\quant{X}{T_1} T_2}}

where
T=\forall X<:T_{1}.\ T_{2}

Before we can use the I.H., we must show that
\Gamma^{\prime},X<:T_{1}
is indeed well-formed.
The only rule that could have been used for well-formedness of the environment
\Gamma,X<:T_{1}

Env-TV

\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{1}\ \text{type}

\Gamma

\Gamma^{\prime},X<:T_{1}\vdash T_{2}\ \text{type}
.
The conclusion then follows from
Type-TAbs

\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{1}=:=\forall X<:T_{11}.\ T_{12}

\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{2}<:T_{11}

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T_1'}{T_1} \quad\subtypesTo{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T_1'}}{T_2}{T_2'}\end{gathered}}{\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{(\quant{X}{T_1} T_2)}{(\quant{X}{T_1'} T_2')}}

•

T-Abs

The approach for this case is the same as the one for the right premise in
T-Let
.

•

T-TAbs

The approach for this case is analogous to the one for
T-Abs
,
the difference being that
Env-TV
is used in the well-formedness derivation instead of
Env-V
.

•

T-TApp

(12)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_1}{\quant{X}{T_1}T_0} \quad\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T_2}{T_1}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_1\ T_2}{(\quant{X}{T_1 T_0})\ T_2}}

where
t=t_{1}\ T_{2}\quad T=(\forall X<:T_{1}T_{0}.\ )\ T_{2}

By the I.H. on the left premise, we have
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{1}:\forall X<:T_{1}.\ T_{0}
.
By part (2) of the lemma, we have
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{2}<:T_{1}
.
The result then follows from
T-TApp
.

•

T-Match

(13)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_s}{T_s} \quad\forall i \in I : \typesTo{\Gamma, x_i : T_i}{t_i}{T_i'} \quad\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T_s}{\cup_{i \in I} T_i}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{\match{t_s}{x_i : T_i \Rightarrow t_i}{i \in I}}{\match{T_s}{T_i \Rightarrow T_i'}{i \in I}}}

where
t=t_{s}\texttt{ match }\{x_{i}:T_{i}\Rightarrow t_{i}\}_{i\in I}\quad T=T_{s}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}

By the I.H.,
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{s}:T_{s}
.
Using the approach outlined in the previous cases, we show that
\vdash\Gamma^{\prime},x_{i}:T_{i}\ \text{env}
.
We then use the I.H. to show
\forall i\in I:\Gamma^{\prime},x_{i}:T_{i}\vdash t_{i}:T_{i}^{\prime}
.
By part (2) of the lemma, we have
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{s}<:\cup_{i\in I}T_{i}
,
after which the result follows from
T-Match
.

∎

B.2.
Weakening

Lemma B.4 (Weakening).

(1)

If
\Gamma\vdash T\ \text{type}
and
\vdash\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime}\ \text{env}
, then
\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime}\vdash T\ \text{type}
.

(2)

If
\Gamma\vdash T\ \text{type}
and
\vdash\Gamma,x:T^{\prime}\ \text{env}
, then
\Gamma,x<:T^{\prime}\vdash T\ \text{type}
.

(3)

If
\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}
and
\vdash\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime}\ \text{env}
, then
\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime}\vdash T<:T^{\prime}
.

(4)

If
\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}
and
\vdash\Gamma,x:T^{\prime\prime}\ \text{env}
, then
\Gamma,x:T^{\prime\prime}\vdash T<:T^{\prime}
.

(5)

If
\Gamma\vdash t:T
and
\vdash\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime}\ \text{env}
, then
\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime}\vdash t:T
.

(6)

If
\Gamma\vdash t:T
and
\vdash\Gamma,x:T^{\prime}\ \text{env}
, then
\Gamma,x:T^{\prime}\vdash t:T
.

Proof.

(1) and (3) are proven simultaneously by induction on the two derivations
\Gamma\vdash T\ \text{type}
and
\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}
.

(1)

By induction on the derivation of
\Gamma\vdash T\ \text{type}
.

•

Type-Int
,
Type-Bool
,
Type-Unit
,
Type-String
,
Type-Bytes

The conclusion is immediate by using
\vdash\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime}\ \text{env}
with the relevant rule.

Type-TV

(14)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\envJ{\Gamma', \subtypes{X'}{T''}}\end{gathered}}{\typeJ{\Gamma', \subtypes{X'}{T''}}{X'}}

where
T=X^{\prime}\quad\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},X^{\prime}<:T^{\prime\prime}

We know that
X\neq X^{\prime}
, as otherwise
(\Gamma^{\prime},X^{\prime}<:T^{\prime\prime},X<:T^{\prime})
would not be well-formed.
Then, we can simply use the same rule
Type-TV
with the new environment to get the desired result.

T=X^{\prime}

(\Gamma,X^{\prime}<:T_{1})

{\Gamma,X^{\prime}<:T_{1},X<:T^{\prime}\vdash T_{2}\ \text{type}}

Type-TAbs

where
T=T_{1}\ T_{2}

{\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime}\vdash T_{1}\ \text{type}}
,
{\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime}\vdash T_{2}\ \text{type}}

and

.

{\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime}\vdash T_{1}=:=\forall X^{\prime}<:T_{11}.\ T_{12}}

and
{\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime}\vdash T_{2}<:T_{11}}
.
Then we simply use
Type-TApp
to obtain the conclusion.

B.3.
Strengthening

Theorem B.5 (Strengthening).

If
\Gamma,x\vdash T^{\prime}:\vdash T<:T^{\prime}
, then
\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}

Proof.

Immediate from inspection of the subtyping rules, as none of them are related to typing of term-level variables.
∎

B.4.
Variable absence

Lemma B.6 (Variable absence).

If
\vdash\Gamma,X<:T,\Gamma^{\prime}\ \text{env}
, then
X
does not appear anywhere in
\Gamma
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
\vdash\Gamma,X<:T,\Gamma^{\prime}\ \text{env}
.

•

Env-
\emptyset

Could not have been used, as the environment is non-empty.

•

Env-V

(33)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typeJ{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T}, \Gamma''}{T'} \quad x \notin dom(\Gamma)\end{gathered}}{\envJ{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T}, \Gamma'', \types{x}{T'}}}

where
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime},x:T^{\prime\prime}

By the definition of the type well-formedness judgement, we have that
\vdash\Gamma,X<:T,\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\ \text{env}
.
Then, by the I.H., we know that
X
does not appear anywhere in
\Gamma
.

•

Env-TV

From the rule, we have
\vdash\Gamma,X<:T,\Gamma^{\prime}\ \text{env}
.
There are two possibilities:

–

\Gamma^{\prime}=\emptyset

In this case, we have from the premises that
\Gamma\vdash T\ \text{type}
and
X\notin dom(\Gamma)
.
If
X
is not in
dom(\Gamma)
, then it cannot appear
anywhere
in
\Gamma
, as otherwise
X
would be unbound.

–

\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime}<:T^{\prime}

In this case, we have from the premises that
\Gamma,X<:T,\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash T^{\prime}\ \text{type}
.
By the definition of the type well-formedness judgement, we have that
\vdash\Gamma,X<:T,\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\ \text{env}
.
Then, by the I.H., we know that
X
does not appear anywhere in
\Gamma
.

∎

B.5.
Narrowing

Lemma B.7 (Narrowing).

(1)

If
\vdash\Gamma,X<:T,\Gamma^{\prime}\ \text{env}
and
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}<:T
, then
\vdash\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\ \text{env}
.

(2)

If
\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T\ \text{type}
and
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime}<:T^{\prime}
, then
\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T\ \text{type}
.

(3)

If
\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T<:T^{\prime}
and
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime\prime}<:T^{\prime\prime}
, then
\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T<:T^{\prime}
.

(4)

If
\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t:
and
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime}<:T^{\prime}
, then
\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t:
.

Proof.

(1), (2) and (3) are proven simultaneously by induction on the derivations

\vdash\Gamma,X<:T,\Gamma^{\prime}\ \text{env}
,
\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T\ \text{type}
and
\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}
.

•

By induction on the derivation of
\vdash\Gamma,X<:T,\Gamma^{\prime}\ \text{env}
.

–

Env-
\emptyset

Does not apply, as the environment is non-empty.

–

Env-V

(34)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typeJ{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T}, \Gamma'}{T''} \quad x \notin dom(\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T}, \Gamma')\end{gathered}}{\envJ{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T}, \Gamma', \types{x}{T''}}}

By part (2) of the lemma, we have
\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T^{\prime\prime}\ \text{type}
.
The domain does not change, so we simply use
Env-V
to obtain the result.

By the I.H., we have
\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{1}\ \text{type}
,

\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{2}\ \text{type}
and

\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\forall Y<:T_{11}.\ T_{12}\ \text{type}
.

\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{1}=:=\forall X<:T_{11}.\ T_{12}
and

\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{2}<:T_{11}
.
The result then follows from
Type-TApp

ST-Var

(36)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\envJ{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T''}, \Gamma'}\end{gathered}}{\subtypesTo{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T''}, \Gamma'}{Y}{T'}}

where
T=Y

There are three possible cases, depending on the environment:

*

\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime\prime},Y<:T^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}

By part (1) of the lemma, we have
\vdash\Gamma^{\prime\prime},Y<:T^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime},X<:T^{\prime\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\ \text{env}
.
The result follows from
ST-Var
.

*

\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime},Y<:T^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}

Analogous to the previous case.

*

X=Y\quad T^{\prime}=T^{\prime\prime}

By part (1) of the lemma, we have
\vdash\Gamma,Y\vdash T^{\prime\prime\prime}<:,\Gamma^{\prime}\ \text{env}
.
By
ST-Var
, we have
\Gamma,Y\vdash T^{\prime\prime\prime}<:,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash Y<:T^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
By
lemma

B.4
with
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime\prime}<:T^{\prime\prime}
, we have

\Gamma,Y<:T^{\prime\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T^{\prime\prime\prime}<:T^{\prime\prime}
.
Now, from
ST-Trans
we have
\Gamma,Y<:T^{\prime\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash Y<:T^{\prime\prime}
,
which, since
X=Y
and
T^{\prime}=T^{\prime\prime}
, is the desired result.

ST-Match1

(37)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\subtypesTo{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T''}, \Gamma'}{T_s}{T_k} \quad\forall i \in I : i < k \Rightarrow\disjoint{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T''}, \Gamma'}{T_s}{T_i}\end{gathered}}{\eqtypesTo{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T''}, \Gamma'}{\match{T_s}{T_i \Rightarrow T_i'}{i \in I}}{T_k'}}

where
T=T_{s}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}\quad T^{\prime}=T_{k}^{\prime}

By the I.H., we have
\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{s}<:T_{k}
.
We expand the second premise to:

(38)

\displaystyle\forall i\in I:i<k\Rightarrow(\not\exists T_{s}^{\prime}:

\displaystyle\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{s}^{\prime}\ \text{type}

(39)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\Gamma.X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{s}^{\prime}<:T_{s}

(40)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{s}^{\prime}<:T_{i})

By part (2) of the lemma, we have
\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{s}^{\prime}\ \text{type}
.
Then we can use the I.H. to show:

(41)

\displaystyle\forall i\in I:i<k\Rightarrow(\not\exists T_{s}^{\prime}:

\displaystyle\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{s}^{\prime}\ \text{type}

(42)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{s}^{\prime}<:T_{s}

(43)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{s}^{\prime}<:T_{i})

The result follows from
ST-Match1
.

where
T=(\forall Y<:T_{1}.\ T_{2})\quad T^{\prime}=(\forall Y<:T_{1}^{\prime}.\ T_{2}^{\prime})

The conclusion is obtained by applying the I.H. on the premises
(using the environment
(\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime},Y<:T_{1}^{\prime})
with the right premise)
and then applying
ST-TAbs

t=t_{1}\ T_{2}\quad T=(\forall X<:T_{1}.\ T_{0})\ T_{2}

\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{1}:\forall X^{\prime}<:T_{1}.\ T_{0}

T-Match

(50)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T'}, \Gamma'}{t_s}{T_s} \quad\forall i \in I : \typesTo{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T'}, \Gamma', \types{x_i}{T_i}}{t_i}{T_i'} \\
\subtypesTo{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T'}, \Gamma'}{T_s}{\cup_{i \in I} T_i}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T'}, \Gamma'}{\match{t_s}{x_i : T_i \Rightarrow t_i}{i \in I}}{\match{T_s}{T_i \Rightarrow T_i'}{i \in I}}}

where
t=t_{s}\texttt{ match }\{x_{i}:T_{i}\Rightarrow t_{i}\}_{i\in I}\quad T=T_{s}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}

By applying the I.H. on the first two premises
(using the environment
\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime},x_{i}:T_{i}
on each of the case derivations)
and by part (3) of the lemma on the last premise, the result follows from
T-Match
.

∎

B.6.
Substitution

Theorem B.8 (Substitution).

(1)

If
{\vdash\Gamma,X<:T,\Gamma^{\prime}\ \text{env}}
and
{\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}<:T}
,

then
{\vdash\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime}]\ \text{env}}
.

(2)

If
{\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T\ \text{type}}
and
{\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime}<:T^{\prime}}
,

then
{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\vdash T[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\ \text{type}}
.

(3)

If
{\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T<:T^{\prime}}
and
{\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime\prime}<:T^{\prime\prime}}
,

then
{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]\vdash T[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]<:T^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]}
.

(4)

If
{\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t:T}
and
{\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime}<:T^{\prime}}
,

then
{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\vdash t[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]:T[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]}
.

(5)

If
{\Gamma,x:T^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t:T}
and
{\Gamma\vdash v:T^{\prime}}
,

then
{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t[x\mapsto v]:T}

Proof.

(1), (2) and (3) are proven simultaneously by induction on the derivations

{\vdash\Gamma,X<:T,\Gamma^{\prime}\ \text{env}}
,
{\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T\ \text{type}}

and
{\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T<:T^{\prime}}
.

(1)

By induction on the derivation of
{\vdash\Gamma,X<:T,\Gamma^{\prime}\ \text{env}}
.

•

Env-
\emptyset

Could not have been used, as the environment is not empty.

•

Env-V

(51)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typeJ{\Gamma', \subtypes{X}{T}, \Gamma''}{T''} \quad x \notin dom(\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T}, \Gamma'')\end{gathered}}{\envJ{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T}, \Gamma'', \types{x}{T''}}}

where
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime},x:T^{\prime\prime}

By using part (2) of the lemma, we have
{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime}]\vdash T^{\prime\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime}]\ \text{type}}
.
None of the term-variables in
\Gamma^{\prime}
are altered by substitution, i.e.

(52)

x\notin dom(\Gamma,X<:T,\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\Rightarrow x\notin dom(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime}])

The result follows from
Env-V
.

•

Env-TV

(53)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typeJ{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T}, \Gamma''}{T''} \quad X' \notin dom(\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T}, \Gamma'')\end{gathered}}{\envJ{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T}, \Gamma'', \subtypes{X'}{T''}}}

where
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime}<:T^{\prime\prime}

By using part (2) of the lemma, we have
{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[X\mapsto T]\vdash T^{\prime\prime}\ \text{type}}
.
We know that
X\neq X^{\prime}
(by the right premise), so

(54)

X^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma,X<:T,\Gamma^{\prime})\Rightarrow X^{\prime}\notin dom(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime}])

The result follows from
Env-TV
.

(2)

By induction on the derivation of
{\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T\ \text{type}}
.

Type-TV

(55)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\envJ{\Gamma, \subtypes{X'}{T}, \Gamma'}\end{gathered}}{\typeJ{\Gamma, \subtypes{X'}{T}, \Gamma'}{X'}}

We know that
X\neq X^{\prime}
, as otherwise we could use part (1) of lemma to obtain

\vdash\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X^{\prime}\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\ \text{env}
, an environment in which
X^{\prime}
is unbound.

Then, either
X<:T^{\prime}\in\Gamma
or
X<:T^{\prime}\in\Gamma^{\prime}
.

If
X\in\Gamma
, there is a
\Gamma_{1}
and
\Gamma_{2}
such that

(56)

\vdash\Gamma_{1},X<:T^{\prime},\Gamma_{2},X^{\prime}<:T,\Gamma^{\prime}\ \text{env}

Then by part (1) of the lemma, we have

(57)

\vdash\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}],X^{\prime}<:T[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}],\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\ \text{env}

The result then follows from
Type-TV
.

If
X\in\Gamma^{\prime}
, there is a
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}
such that

(58)

\vdash\Gamma,X^{\prime}<:T,\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},X<:T^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}\ \text{env}

Then by the 1st part of the lemma, we have

(59)

\vdash\Gamma,X^{\prime}<:T,\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\ \text{env}

The result then also follows from
Type-TV
.

Type-TApp

(61)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typeJ{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T'}, \Gamma'}{T_1} \quad\typeJ{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T'}, \Gamma'}{T_2}\\
\eqtypesTo{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T'}, \Gamma'}{T_1}{\quant{X'}{T_{11}} T_{12}} \quad\typeJ{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T'}, \Gamma'}{\quant{X'}{T_{11}} T_{12}} \quad\subtypesTo{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T'}, \Gamma'}{T_2}{T_{11}}\end{gathered}}{\typeJ{\Gamma, \subtypes{X}{T'}, \Gamma'}{T_1\ T_2}}

where
T=T_{1}\ T_{2}

By the I.H.,
{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\vdash T_{1}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\ \text{type}}
,
{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\vdash T_{2}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\ \text{type}}

and
{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\vdash\forall X^{\prime}<:T_{11}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}].\ T_{12}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\ \text{type}}
.
Then, from part (3) of the lemma, we have

{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\vdash T_{1}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]=:=\forall X^{\prime}<:T_{11}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}].\ T_{12}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]}

and
{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\vdash T_{2}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]<:T_{1}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]}
.
Finally, we just use
Type-TApp
to obtain the conclusion.

(3)

By induction on the derivation of
{\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T<:T^{\prime}}
.

ST-Val

where
T=\underline{v_{G}}

Since
v_{G}
is a ground value,
\underline{v_{G}}=\underline{v_{G}}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]
.
Now, we have to consider if
v_{G}\mathrel{\in_{G}}T^{\prime}\Rightarrow v_{G}\mathrel{\in_{G}}T^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]
.
Looking at the rules for
\in_{G}
, it is clear that type substitution has no effect on the judgement,
so the conclusion follows from
ST-Val
.

ST-Var

(62)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\envJ{\Gamma_1, \subtypes{X'}{T'}, \Gamma_2}\end{gathered}}{\subtypesTo{\Gamma_1, \subtypes{X'}{T'}, \Gamma_2}{X'}{T'}}

where
T=X^{\prime}

Either
X<:T^{\prime\prime}\in\Gamma_{1}
or
X<:T^{\prime\prime}\in\Gamma_{2}
.

If
X<:T^{\prime\prime}\in\Gamma_{1}
, then there is a
\Gamma_{11}
and
\Gamma_{12}
such that

(63)

\vdash\Gamma_{11},X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma_{12},X^{\prime}<:T^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}\ \text{env}

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{11}\quad\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{12},X^{\prime}<:T^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}

By part (1) of the lemma, we have

(64)

\vdash\Gamma_{11},\Gamma_{12}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}],X^{\prime}<:T^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}],\Gamma_{2}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]\ \text{env}

The result then follows from
ST-Var
(
X=X[X^{\prime}\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]
).

If
X<:T^{\prime\prime}\in\Gamma_{2}
, then there is a
\Gamma_{21}
and
\Gamma_{22}
such that

(65)

\vdash\Gamma_{1},X^{\prime}<:T^{\prime},\Gamma_{21},X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma_{22}\ \text{env}

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},X^{\prime}<:T^{\prime},\Gamma_{21}\quad\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{22}

By part (1) of the lemma, we have

(66)

\vdash\Gamma_{1},X^{\prime}<:T^{\prime},\Gamma_{21},\Gamma_{22}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]\ \text{env}

Then from
ST-Var
, we can show
\Gamma_{1},X^{\prime}<:T^{\prime},\Gamma_{21},\Gamma_{22}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]\vdash X^{\prime}<:T^{\prime}
.
We know that
X^{\prime}=X^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]
and by
lemma

B.6
, that
T^{\prime}=T^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]
, so

\Gamma_{1},X^{\prime}<:T^{\prime},\Gamma_{21},\Gamma_{22}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]\vdash X^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]<:T^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]
,
which is what we wanted to show.

ST-Match1

where
T={T_{s}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}}\quad T^{\prime}={T_{k}^{\prime}}

By the I.H. on the first premise, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]\vdash T_{s}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]<:T_{k}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]
.
We expand the second premise to:

(67)

\displaystyle\forall i\in I:i<k\Rightarrow(\not\exists T_{s}^{\prime}:

\displaystyle\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{s}^{\prime}\ \text{type}

(68)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\Gamma.X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{s}^{\prime}<:T_{s}

(69)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash T_{s}^{\prime}<:T_{i})

By part (2) of the lemma, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]\vdash

type
.
Then we can use the I.H. to show:

(70)

\displaystyle\forall i\in I:i<k\Rightarrow(\not\exists T_{s}^{\prime}:

\displaystyle\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]\vdash T_{s}^{\prime}\ \text{type}

(71)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]\vdash T_{s}^{\prime}<:T_{s}

(72)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]\vdash T_{s}^{\prime}<:T_{i})

The result follows from
ST-Match1
.

•

ST-TAbs

where
T=\forall X^{\prime}<:T_{1}.\ T_{2}\quad T^{\prime}=\forall X^{\prime}<:T_{1}^{\prime}.\ {T_{2}^{\prime}}

By the I.H. (with the environment
\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime},X^{\prime}<:T_{1}^{\prime}
in the right premise), we have that

{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]\vdash T_{1}^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]<:T_{1}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]}

and
{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}],X^{\prime}<:T_{1}^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]\vdash T_{2}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]<:T_{2}^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime\prime}]}
.
The result follows from
ST-TAbs
.

(4)

By induction on the derivation of
{\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t:T}
.

where
t=v\quad T=\underline{v}
.

v=v[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]
and
\underline{v}=\underline{v}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]
,
so the result is immediate from using
T-Val
with the new environment.

T-Var

(73)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\envJ{\Gamma_1, \types{x}{T}, \Gamma_2}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma_1, \types{x}{T}, \Gamma_2}{x}{T}}

Either
X<:T^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{1}
or
X<:T^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{2}
.

If
X<:T^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{1}
, then there is a
\Gamma_{11}
and
\Gamma_{12}
such that

(74)

\vdash\Gamma_{11},X<:T^{\prime},\Gamma_{12},x:T,\Gamma_{2}\ \text{env}

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{11}\quad\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{12},x:T,\Gamma_{2}

By part (1) of the lemma:

(75)

\vdash\Gamma_{11},\Gamma_{12}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}],x:T[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}],\Gamma_{2}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\ \text{env}

The result then follows from
T-Var
(since
x=x[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]
).
If
X<:T^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{2}
, then there is a
\Gamma_{21}
and
\Gamma_{22}
such that

(76)

\vdash\Gamma_{1},x:T,\Gamma_{21},X<:T^{\prime},\Gamma_{22}\ \text{env}

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},x:T,\Gamma_{21}\quad\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{22}

By part (1) of the lemma:

(77)

\vdash\Gamma_{1},x:T,\Gamma_{21},\Gamma_{22}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\ \text{env}

From
T-Var
, we have
\Gamma_{1},x:T,\Gamma_{21},\Gamma_{22}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\vdash x:T
.
By
lemma

B.6
, we know that
X
is not in
T
, so
T=T[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]
.
This gives us
\Gamma_{1},x:T,\Gamma_{21},\Gamma_{22}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\vdash x[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]:T[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]
,
which is what we wanted to show.

•

T-Let

By the I.H. (using the environment
\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime},x:T_{0}
on the right premise),
we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\vdash t_{0}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]:T_{0}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]

and
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}],x\vdash T_{0}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]:\vdash t_{1}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]:T_{1}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]
.

T-Let
.

),

By the I.H., we have

.

T-Match

By the I.H. (using the environment
\Gamma,X<:T^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime},x_{i}:T_{i}
on the right premise),
we have
\Gamma,\Gamma[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\vdash t_{s}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]:T_{s}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]

and
\forall i\in I:\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}],x_{i}:T_{i}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\vdash t_{i}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]:T_{i}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]
.

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\vdash T_{s}<:\cup_{i\in I}T_{i}
,
and the result then follows from
T-Match
.

•

T-Sub

By the I.H. on the left premise, we have
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\vdash t[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]:T^{\prime\prime\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]
.
By part (3) of the lemma,
\Gamma,\Gamma[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]\vdash T^{\prime\prime\prime}[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]<:T[X\mapsto T^{\prime\prime}]
.
The result follows from
T-Sub
.

(5)

By induction on the derivation of
{\Gamma,x:T^{\prime}\vdash t:T}
.

.

.

B.7.
Exclusivity

Lemma B.9 (Exclusivity).

Subtyping (
\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}
) and disjointness (
\Gamma\vdash T\diamond T^{\prime}
) are mutually exclusive.

(1)

\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}\Rightarrow\Gamma\not\vdash T\diamond T^{\prime}

(2)

\Gamma\vdash T\diamond T^{\prime}\Rightarrow\Gamma\not\vdash T<:T^{\prime}

Proof.

To reiterate, the expanded definition of disjointness is:

(79)

\not\exists T^{\prime\prime}:\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime}\ \text{type}\wedge\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime}<:T\wedge\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime}<:T^{\prime}

(1)

\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}\Rightarrow\Gamma\not\vdash T\diamond T^{\prime}

If
\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}
, then we know that
\Gamma\vdash T\ \text{type}
.
From
ST-Refl
, we have:

(80)

\Gamma\vdash T\ \text{type}\wedge\Gamma\vdash T<:T\wedge\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}

which contradicts the definition of disjointness.

(2)

\Gamma\vdash T\diamond T^{\prime}\Rightarrow\Gamma\not\vdash T<:T^{\prime}

Suppose for the sake of contradiction that
\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}
.
We then have that
\Gamma\vdash T\ \text{type}
, and from
ST-Refl
:

(81)

\Gamma\vdash T\ \text{type}\wedge\Gamma\vdash T<:T\wedge\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}

which contradicts our initial assumption, so it must be the case that
\Gamma\not\vdash T<:T^{\prime}
.

∎

B.8.
Minimal types

Lemma B.10 (Minimal types).

(1)

If
\Gamma\vdash v_{G}:T
, then
\Gamma\vdash\underline{v_{G}}<:T

(2)

If
\Gamma\vdash\lambda x:T_{1}.\ t_{2}:T
, then there is a
T_{2}
such that
\Gamma\vdash T_{1}\rightarrow T_{2}<:T

(3)

If
\Gamma\vdash\char 12\relax{X}{T_{1}}t_{2}:T
, then there is a
T_{2}
such that
\Gamma\vdash\forall X<:T_{1}.\ T_{2}<:T

Proof.

(1)

By induction on the derivation of
\Gamma\vdash v_{G}:T
.

•

T-Val

where
T=\underline{v_{G}}

Immediate from
ST-Refl
.

•

T-Sub

(82)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{v_G}{T'} \quad\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T'}{T}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{v_G}{T}}

By the I.H., we have that
\Gamma\vdash\underline{v_{G}}<:T^{\prime}
.
We obtain the result by applying
ST-Trans
.

•

The rest of the rules are not applicable to ground values.

(2)

By induction on the derivation of
\Gamma\vdash\lambda x:T_{1}.\ t_{2}:T
.

•

T-Abs

where
T=T_{1}\rightarrow T_{2}

Immediate from
ST-Refl
.

•

T-Sub

(83)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{\abs{x}{T_1} t_2}{T'} \quad\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T'}{T}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{\abs{x}{T_1} t_2}{T}}

By the I.H., we have that there is a
T_{2}
such that
\Gamma\vdash T_{1}\rightarrow T_{2}<:T^{\prime}
.
We obtain the result by applying
ST-Trans
.

•

The rest of the rules are not applicable to term abstractions.

(3)

By induction on the derivation of
\Gamma\vdash\char 12\relax{X}{T_{1}}t_{2}:T
.

•

T-TAbs

where
T=\forall X<:T_{1}.\ T_{2}

Immediate from
ST-Refl
.

T-Sub

(84)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{\tabs{X}{T_1} t_2}{T'} \quad\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T'}{T}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{\tabs{X}{T_1} t_2}{T}}

By the I.H., we have that there is a
T_{2}
such that
\Gamma\vdash\forall X<:T_{1}.\ T_{2}<:T^{\prime}

We obtain the result by applying
ST-Trans

The rest of the rules are not applicable to type abstractions.

B.9.
Inversion

We first introduce the following relation

\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\begin{gathered}\Rule{\vdots}
{\eqtypesTo{\Gamma}{T_1}{T_2}}
{ST-App}\end{gathered}\end{gathered}}{\mutualTo{\Gamma}{T_1}{T_2}}\ \textsc{M-TApp}\qquad\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\begin{gathered}\Rule{\vdots}
{\eqtypesTo{\Gamma}{T_1}{T_2}}
{ST-Match1}\end{gathered}\end{gathered}}{\mutualTo{\Gamma}{T_1}{T_2}}\ \textsc{M-Match}

\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\begin{gathered}\mutualTo{\Gamma}{T_1}{T_2} \quad\mutualTo{\Gamma}{T_2}{T_3}\end{gathered}\end{gathered}}{\mutualTo{\Gamma}{T_1}{T_3}}\ \textsc{M-Trans}

The relation
\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons T^{\prime}
shows mutual subtyping between types
T
and
T^{\prime}
,
with the constraint that
T
must be either a match type or a type application, and that
the derivation is constructed with pairwise application of
ST-App
,
ST-Match1

and
ST-Trans
.

Lemma B.11 (Subtyping inversion).

We prove a number of properties regarding subtyping inversion:

(1)

If
\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons T^{\prime}
, then either:

(a)

T=T_{s}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}
,
\Gamma\vdash T_{s}<:T_{k}
,

\forall i\in I:i<k\Rightarrow\Gamma\vdash T_{s}\diamond T_{i}
and

\Gamma\vdash T_{k}^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons T^{\prime}
,

(b)

T=T_{s}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}
,
\Gamma\vdash T_{s}<:T_{k}
,

\forall i\in I:i<k\Rightarrow\Gamma\vdash T_{s}\diamond T_{i}
and
T_{k}^{\prime}=T^{\prime}
,

(c)

T=(\forall X<:T_{1}.\ T_{0})\ T_{2}
,
\Gamma\vdash T_{2}<:T_{1}
and

\Gamma\vdash T_{0}[X\mapsto T_{2}]\rightleftharpoons T^{\prime}
,

(d)

T=(\forall X<:T_{1}.\ T_{0})\ T_{2}
,
\Gamma\vdash T_{2}<:T_{1}
and

T_{0}[X\mapsto T_{2}]=T^{\prime}
.

(2)

If
\Gamma\vdash T<:\underline{v_{G}}
, or
\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}
where
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons\underline{v_{G}}
,
then either:

(a)

\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons\underline{v_{G}}
,

(b)

T
is ground type.

(3)

If
\Gamma\vdash T<:X
, or
\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}
where
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons X
,
then either:

(a)

\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons Y
for some type variable
Y
,

(b)

T
is a type variable.

(4)

If
\Gamma\vdash T<:T_{1}^{\prime}\rightarrow T_{2}^{\prime}

or
\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}
and
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons T_{1}^{\prime}\rightarrow T_{2}^{\prime}
then either:

(a)

\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons T_{1}\rightarrow T_{2}
for some
T_{1}
and
T_{2}

such that
\Gamma\vdash T_{1}^{\prime}<:T_{1}
and
\Gamma\vdash T_{2}<:T_{2}^{\prime}
,

(b)

\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons X
for some type variable
X
,

(c)

T
is a type variable,

(d)

T=T_{1}\rightarrow T_{2}
where
\Gamma\vdash T_{1}^{\prime}<:T_{1}
and
\Gamma\vdash T_{2}<:T_{2}^{\prime}
.

(5)

If
\Gamma\vdash T<:(\forall X<:T_{1}^{\prime}.\ T_{2}^{\prime})
or
\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}

and
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons(\forall X<:T_{1}^{\prime}.\ T_{2}^{\prime})
, then either:

(a)

\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons(\forall X<:T_{1}.\ T_{2})
, for some
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
such that

\Gamma\vdash T_{1}^{\prime}<:T_{1}
and
\Gamma,X<:T_{1}^{\prime}\vdash T_{2}<:T_{2}^{\prime}
,

(b)

\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons X
, for some type variable
X
,

(c)

T
is a type variable,

(d)

T=\forall X<:T_{1}^{\prime\prime}.\ {T_{2}}
and
\Gamma,X<:T_{1}^{\prime\prime}\vdash T_{2}<:T_{2}^{\prime}
.

(6)

If
\Gamma\vdash T<:\{f_{i}:T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}
or
\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}

and
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons\{f_{i}:T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}
, then either:

(a)

\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons\{f_{i}:T_{i}\}_{i\in I}
such that

\forall i\in I:\Gamma\vdash T_{i}<:T_{i}^{\prime}
.

(b)

\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons X
, for some type variable
X
,

(c)

T
is a type variable,

(d)

\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons\underline{v_{G}}
for some ground type
\underline{v_{G}}
,

(e)

T
is a ground type,

(f)

T=\{f_{i}:T_{i}\}
and
\forall i\in I:\Gamma\vdash T_{i}<:T_{i}^{\prime}
.

(7)

If
\Gamma\vdash T<:[T_{l}^{\prime}]
or
\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}

and
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons[T_{l}^{\prime}]
, then either:

(a)

\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons[T_{l}]
, for some
T_{l}
such that

\Gamma\vdash T_{l}<:T_{l}^{\prime}
.

(b)

\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons X
, for some type variable
X
,

(c)

T
is a type variable,

(d)

\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons\underline{v_{G}}
for some ground type
\underline{v_{G}}
,

(e)

T
is a ground type,

(f)

T=[T_{l}]
and
\Gamma\vdash T_{l}<:T_{l}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

All proofs are by induction on the size of the derivation.

(1)

\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons T^{\prime}

•

M-TApp

where
T=(\forall X<:T_{1}.\ T_{0})\ T_{2}\quad T^{\prime}=T_{0}[X\mapsto T_{2}]

We have case
d
immediately from the premises.

•

M-Match

where
T=T_{s}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}\quad T^{\prime}=T_{k}^{\prime}

We have case
b
immediately from the premises.

•

M-Trans

(85)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\mutualTo{\Gamma}{T}{T''} \quad\mutualTo{\Gamma}{T''}{T'}\end{gathered}}{\mutualTo{\Gamma}{T}{T'}}

By the I.H. on the left premise, we have four subcases:

(a)

T=T_{s}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}
,
\Gamma\vdash T_{s}<:T_{k}
,

\forall i\in I:i<k\Rightarrow\Gamma\vdash T_{s}\diamond T_{i}
and

\Gamma\vdash T_{k}^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons T^{\prime\prime}
.

By
M-Trans
we have case
a
.

(b)

T=T_{s}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}
,
\Gamma\vdash T_{s}<:T_{k}
,

\forall i\in I:i<k\Rightarrow\Gamma\vdash T_{s}\diamond T_{i}
and
T_{k}^{\prime}=T^{\prime\prime}

We immediately have case
a
.

(c)

T=(\forall X<:T_{1}.\ T_{0})\ T_{2}
,
\Gamma\vdash T_{2}<:T_{1}
and

\Gamma\vdash T_{0}[X\mapsto T_{2}]\rightleftharpoons T^{\prime\prime}
.

By
M-Trans
we have case
c
.

(d)

T=(\forall X<:T_{1}.\ T_{0})\ T_{2}
,
\Gamma\vdash T_{2}<:T_{1}
and

T_{0}[X\mapsto T_{2}]=T^{\prime\prime}
.
We immediately have case
c
.

(2)

\Gamma\vdash T<:T^{\prime}

ST-Refl

where
T=T^{\prime}

If
T^{\prime}
is a ground type, we immediately have case
b
.
If
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons\underline{v_{G}}
, then we have case
a
.

ST-Val

where
T=\underline{v_{G}}

Case
b
is immediate.

•

ST-Var

From looking at the rules for the "
\in_{G}
" relation, it is clear that the type on the right-hand
side cannot be a ground type, so this rule could not have been used.

•

ST-Trans

(86)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T}{T''} \quad\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T''}{T'}\end{gathered}}{\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T}{T'}}

If
T^{\prime}
is a ground type
\underline{v_{G}}
, then we can use the I.H. on the right premise
to get that either
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime}\rightleftharpoons\underline{v_{G}}
or
T^{\prime\prime}
is a ground type.
In any case, we can use the I.H. on the left premise to obtain the result.
If
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons\underline{v_{G}}
we can also use the I.H. on the right, then
left premise to obtain the result.

•

ST-Match1

First case:

(87)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T_s}{T_k} \quad\forall i \in I : i < k \Rightarrow\disjoint{\Gamma}{T_s}{T_i}\end{gathered}}{\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{\match{T_s}{T_i \Rightarrow T_i'}{i \in I}}{T_k'}}

where
T=T_{s}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}\quad T^{\prime}=T_{k}^{\prime}

If
T^{\prime}=\underline{v_{G}}
, we have case
a
from
M-Match
.

If
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons\underline{v_{G}}
, we have case
a
from
M-Match
and
M-Trans
.

Second case:

(88)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T_s}{T_k} \quad\forall i \in I : i < k \Rightarrow\disjoint{\Gamma}{T_s}{T_i}\end{gathered}}{\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T_k'}{\match{T_s}{T_i \Rightarrow T_i'}{i \in I}}}

where
T=T_{k}^{\prime}\quad T^{\prime}=T_{s}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}

We use the 1st part of the lemma on
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons\underline{v_{G}}
to get four subcases:

(a)

T^{\prime}=T_{s}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}
,
\Gamma\vdash T_{s}<:T_{k}
,

\forall i\in I:i<k\Rightarrow\Gamma\vdash T_{s}\diamond T_{i}
and

\Gamma\vdash T_{k}^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons\underline{v_{G}}

By
T=T_{k}^{\prime}
we have
\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons\underline{v_{G}}
, which gives us case
a
.

(b)

T^{\prime}=T_{s}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}
,
\Gamma\vdash T_{s}<:T_{k}
,

\forall i\in I:i<k\Rightarrow\Gamma\vdash T_{s}\diamond T_{i}
and
T_{k}^{\prime}=\underline{v_{G}}

Case
b
is immediate.

(c)

Not applicable since
T^{\prime}
is a match type.

(d)

Not applicable since
T^{\prime}
is a match type.

•

ST-Match2

(89)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T_s}{T_s'} \quad\forall i \in I : \subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T_i'}{T_i''}\end{gathered}}{\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{\match{T_s}{T_i \Rightarrow T_i'}{i \in I}}{\match{T_s'}{T_i \Rightarrow T_i''}{i \in I}}}

where
T=T_{s}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}\quad T^{\prime}=T_{s}^{\prime}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime\prime}\}_{i\in I}

We use the 1st part of the lemma on
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons\underline{v_{G}}
to get four subcases:

(a)

T^{\prime}=T_{s}^{\prime}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime\prime}\}_{i\in I}
,
\Gamma\vdash T_{s}^{\prime}<:T_{k}
,

\forall i\in I:i<k\Rightarrow\Gamma\vdash T_{s}^{\prime}\diamond T_{i}
and

\Gamma\vdash T_{k}^{\prime\prime}\rightleftharpoons\underline{v_{G}}

By the definition of disjointness and
ST-Trans
, we have

\forall i\in I:i<k\Rightarrow\Gamma\vdash T_{s}\diamond T_{i}
.
Also from
ST-Trans
, we have
\Gamma\vdash T_{s}<:T_{k}
.
Then, from
ST-Match1
and
M-Match
, we have
\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons T_{k}^{\prime}
.
Now, since
\Gamma\vdash T_{k}^{\prime}<:T_{k}^{\prime\prime}
and
\Gamma\vdash T_{k}^{\prime\prime}\rightleftharpoons\underline{v_{G}}
,
we can use the I.H. to get that either
\Gamma\vdash T_{k}^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons\underline{v_{G}}
,
or
T_{k}^{\prime}
is a ground type.
In the first case, we use
M-Trans
to get
\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons\underline{v_{G}}
for case
a
.
In the second case, we have case
a
from
\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons T_{k}^{\prime}
.

(b)

T^{\prime}=T_{s}^{\prime}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime\prime}\}_{i\in I}
,
\Gamma\vdash T_{s}^{\prime}<:T_{k}
,

\forall i\in I:i<k\Rightarrow\Gamma\vdash T_{s}^{\prime}\diamond T_{i}
and
T_{k}^{\prime\prime}=\underline{v_{G}}

By the definition of disjointness and
ST-Trans
, we have

\forall i\in I:i<k\Rightarrow\Gamma\vdash T_{s}\diamond T_{i}
.
Also from
ST-Trans
, we have
\Gamma\vdash T_{s}<:T_{k}
.
Then, from
ST-Match1
and
M-Match
, we have
\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons T_{k}^{\prime}
.
Since
T_{k}^{\prime\prime}=\underline{v_{G}}
, we have
\Gamma\vdash T_{k}^{\prime}<:\underline{v_{G}}
, and then
we can use the I.H. to get that either
\Gamma\vdash T_{k}^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons\underline{v_{G}}
,
or
T_{k}^{\prime}
is a ground type.
In the first case, we use
M-Trans
to get
\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons\underline{v_{G}}
for case
a
.
In the second case, we have case
a
from
\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons T_{k}^{\prime}
.

(c)

Not applicable since
T^{\prime}
is a match type.

(d)

Not applicable since
T^{\prime}
is a match type.

ST-Trans

(92)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T}{T''} \quad\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T''}{T'}\end{gathered}}{\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T}{T'}}

If
T^{\prime}
is a type variable
X
then we use the I.H. on the right premise to get that either

\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime}\rightleftharpoons X
or
T^{\prime\prime}
is a type variable. In any case, we can use the I.H.
on the left premise to obtain the result.

If
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons X
we can also use the I.H. on the right, then left premise to
obtain the result.

(95)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T_s}{T_s'} \quad\forall i \in I : \subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T_i'}{T_i''}\end{gathered}}{\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{\match{T_s}{T_i \Rightarrow T_i'}{i \in I}}{\match{T_s'}{T_i \Rightarrow T_i''}{i \in I}}}

where
T=T_{s}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}\quad T^{\prime}=T_{s}^{\prime}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime\prime}\}_{i\in I}

We use the 1st part of the lemma on
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons X
to get four subcases:

and

\Gamma\vdash T_{k}^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons Y
for some type variable
Y
,
or
T_{k}^{\prime}
is a type variable.
In the first case, we use
M-Trans
to get
\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons Y
for case
a

In the second case, we have case
a
from
\Gamma\vdash T\rightleftharpoons T_{k}^{\prime}

(b)

T^{\prime}=T_{s}^{\prime}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime\prime}\}_{i\in I}
,
\Gamma\vdash T_{s}^{\prime}<:T_{k}
,

\forall i\in I:i<k\Rightarrow\Gamma\vdash T_{s}^{\prime}\diamond T_{i}
and
T_{k}^{\prime\prime}=X

By the definition of disjointness and
ST-Trans
, we have

\forall i\in I:i<k\Rightarrow\Gamma\vdash T_{s}\diamond T_{i}

ST-Trans

(98)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T}{T''} \quad\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T''}{T'}\end{gathered}}{\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T}{T'}}

By the I.H. on the right premise, we have four subcases:

(a)

\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime}\rightleftharpoons T_{1}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow T_{2}^{\prime\prime}
for some
T_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
T_{2}^{\prime\prime}

such that
\Gamma\vdash T_{1}^{\prime}<:T_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
\Gamma\vdash T_{2}^{\prime\prime}<:T_{2}^{\prime}
.

The result follows by using the I.H. on the left premise. If the result is case
a
or
d
,
we have
\Gamma\vdash T_{1}^{\prime}<:T_{1}
and
\Gamma\vdash T_{2}<:T_{2}^{\prime}
by

ST-Trans
.

(b)

\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime}\rightleftharpoons X
for some type variable
X
.

The result follows from using the 3rd part of the lemma on the left premise.

(c)

T^{\prime\prime}
is a type variable.
The result follows from using the 3rd part of the lemma on the left premise.

(d)

T^{\prime\prime}=T_{1}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow T_{2}^{\prime\prime}
for some
T_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
T_{2}^{\prime\prime}

such that
\Gamma\vdash T_{1}^{\prime}<:T_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
\Gamma\vdash T_{2}^{\prime\prime}<:T_{2}^{\prime}
.

The result follows by using the I.H. on the left premise. If the result is case
a
or
d
,
we have
\Gamma\vdash T_{1}^{\prime}<:T_{1}
and
\Gamma\vdash T_{2}<:T_{2}^{\prime}
by

ST-Trans
.

(118)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T}{T''} \quad\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T''}{T'}\end{gathered}}{\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T}{T'}}

By the I.H. on the right premise, we have six subcases:

(a)

\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime}\rightleftharpoons[T_{l}^{\prime\prime}]
such that

\Gamma\vdash T_{l}^{\prime\prime}<:T_{l}^{\prime}

The result follows by using the I.H. on the left premise.

a
or
f
,
we have
\Gamma\vdash T_{l}<:T_{l}^{\prime}
by
ST-Trans
.

(b)

\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime}\rightleftharpoons X
for some type variable
X
.

The result follows from using the 3rd part of the lemma on the left premise.

(c)

T^{\prime\prime}
is a type variable.
The result follows from using the 3rd part of the lemma on the left premise.

(d)

\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime}\rightleftharpoons\underline{v_{G}}
for some ground type
\underline{v_{G}}
.

The result follows from using the 2nd part of the lemma on the left premise.

(e)

T^{\prime\prime}
is a ground type.

The result follows from using the 2nd part of the lemma on the left premise.

(f)

T^{\prime\prime}=[T_{l}^{\prime\prime}]
such that

\Gamma\vdash T_{l}^{\prime\prime}<:T_{l}^{\prime}

The result follows by using the I.H. on the left premise.

a
or

,

These rules could not have been used.

∎

Lemma B.12 (Typing inversion).

(1)

If
\Gamma\vdash\lambda x:T_{1}.\ t_{2}:T
and
\Gamma\vdash T<:T_{1}^{\prime}\rightarrow T_{2}^{\prime}

then
\Gamma\vdash T_{1}^{\prime}<:T_{1}
and there is a
T_{2}
such that
\Gamma,x:T_{1}\vdash t_{2}:T_{2}

and
\Gamma\vdash T_{2}<:T_{2}^{\prime}
.

(2)

If
\Gamma\vdash\char 12\relax{X}{T_{1}}t_{2}:T
and
\Gamma\vdash T<:\forall X<:T_{1}^{\prime}.\ T_{2}^{\prime}

then
T_{1}=T_{1}^{\prime}
and there is a
T_{2}
such that
\Gamma,X<:T_{1}\vdash t_{2}:T_{2}

and
\Gamma,X<:T_{1}\vdash T_{2}<:T_{2}^{\prime}
.

(3)

If
\Gamma\vdash{t}\mathbin{\texttt{{:}\!{:}}}{t^{\prime}}:T
and
\Gamma\vdash T<:[T^{\prime}]

then there is a
T^{\prime\prime}
such that
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime}<:T^{\prime}
,
{\Gamma\vdash t:T^{\prime\prime}}

and
{\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}:[T^{\prime\prime}]}
.

(4)

If
\Gamma\vdash\{f_{i}=t_{i}\}_{i\in I}:T
and
\Gamma\vdash T<:\{f_{i}:T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}

then
\forall i\in I,\exists T_{i}:\Gamma\vdash t_{i}:T_{i}\wedge\Gamma\vdash T_{i}<:T_{i}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

(1)

By induction on the derivation of
\Gamma\vdash\lambda x:T_{1}.\ t_{2}:T
.

•

T-Abs

(124)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma, \types{x}{T_1}}{t_2}{T_2''}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{\abs{x}{T_1} t_2}{T_1 \rightarrow T_2''}}

We have that
\Gamma\vdash T_{1}\rightarrow T_{2}^{\prime\prime}<:T_{1}^{\prime}\rightarrow T_{2}^{\prime}
.
By
lemma

B.11
, we have
\Gamma\vdash T_{1}^{\prime}<:T_{1}
and
\Gamma\vdash T_{2}^{\prime\prime}<:T_{2}^{\prime}
.
We take
T_{2}=T_{2}^{\prime\prime}
to obtain the desired result.

•

T-Sub

(125)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{\abs{x}{T_1} t_2}{T'} \subtypes{\Gamma}{T'}{T}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{\abs{x}{T_1} t_2}{T}}

By
ST-Trans
,
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}<:T_{1}^{\prime}\rightarrow T_{2}^{\prime}
.
The conclusion is then immediate after applying the I.H..

•

In any other case, the term cannot be an abstraction.

(2)

By induction on the derivation of
\Gamma\vdash\char 12\relax{X}{T_{1}}t_{2}:T
.

(\forall X<:T_{1}.\ T_{2}^{\prime\prime})\vdash(\forall X<:T_{1}^{\prime}.\ T_{2}^{\prime})<:

lemma

B.11
, we have

and
\Gamma,X<:T_{1}\vdash T_{2}^{\prime\prime}<:T_{2}^{\prime}
.

T_{2}=T_{2}^{\prime\prime}
to obtain the desired result.

(3)

By induction on the derivation of
\Gamma\vdash{t}\mathbin{\texttt{{:}\!{:}}}{t^{\prime}}:T

T-List

(128)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t}{T'''} \quad\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'}{\listT{T'''}}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{\concat{t}{t'}}{\listT{T'''}}}

\Gamma\vdash[T^{\prime\prime\prime}]<:[T^{\prime}]
.

lemma

B.11
, we have
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime\prime}<:T^{\prime}
,

\Gamma\vdash t:T^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}:[T^{\prime\prime\prime}]
.

T^{\prime\prime}=T^{\prime\prime\prime}
to obtain the desired result.

T-Sub

(129)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{\concat{t}{t'}}{T'''} \quad\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T'''}{T}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{\concat{t}{t'}}{T}}

By
ST-Trans
,
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime\prime\prime}<:[T^{\prime}]
.
The conclusion is then immediate after applying the I.H..

•

In any other case, the term cannot be a list concatenation.

(4)

By induction on the derivation of
\Gamma\vdash\{f_{i}=t_{i}\}_{i\in I}:T
.

•

T-Rec

(130)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\forall i \in I : \typesTo{\Gamma}{t_i}{T_i''}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{\curl{f_i = t_i}_{i \in I}}{\curl{f_i : T_i''}_{i \in I}}}

We have
\Gamma\vdash\{f_{i}:T_{i}^{\prime\prime}\}_{i\in I}<:\{f_{i}:T_{i}\}_{i\in I}
.
By
lemma

B.11
, we have

\forall i\in I:\Gamma\vdash t_{i}:T_{i}^{\prime\prime}\wedge\Gamma\vdash T_{i}^{\prime\prime}<:T_{i}^{\prime}
.
By taking each
T_{i}
to be
T_{i}^{\prime\prime}
, we obtain the conclusion.

•

T-Sub

(131)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{\curl{f_i = t_i}_{i \in I}}{T'} \quad\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T'}{T}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{\curl{f_i = t_i}_{i \in I}}{T}}

By
ST-Trans
,
\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}<:\{f_{i}:T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}
.
The conclusion is then immediate after applying the I.H..

•

In any other case, the term cannot be a record.

∎

Lemma B.13 (Type preservation for
F_{\text{P4R}}
terms).

If
{\Gamma\vdash t:T}
and
{t\rightarrow t^{\prime}}
, then
{\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}:T}

Proof.

The proof is by induction on the derivation of
{\Gamma\vdash t:T}
.

•

T-Val
,
T-Var
,
T-Abs
,
T-TAbs

These rules type values which have no applicable reduction rules,
so the premise is false and the conclusion holds vacuously.

•

T-Rec

(132)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\forall i \in I : \typesTo{\Gamma}{t_i}{T_i}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{\curl{f_i = t_i}_{i \in I}}{\curl{f_i : T_i}_{i \in I}}}

where
t=\{f_{i}=t_{i}\}_{i\in I}\quad T=\{f_{i}:T_{i}\}_{i\in I}

If all fields
t_{i}
are values, there is no reduction rule, so the premise is false.

If there is at least one field which is not a value,
t
must have been reduced using the context reduction rule
E-
\mathbb{C}
.
Let this field be
t_{k}
such that there is no
j\in I,j<k
where
t_{j}
is not a value.
Then the reduction is:

(133)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}t'' \reduce{\alpha} t'''\end{gathered}}{\mbC[t''] \reduce{\alpha} \mbC[t''']}

where
t^{\prime\prime}=t_{k}
,
\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]=t
,
\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime\prime}]=t^{\prime}
and

\mathbb{C}=\{f_{i}=\gamma_{i}\}_{i\in I}\quad\text{where }\begin{array}[]{c}\forall i\in i\end{array}:\begin{cases}i<k\Rightarrow\gamma_{i}=v_{i}\\
i=k\Rightarrow\gamma_{i}=\texttt{[\,]}\\
i>k\Rightarrow\gamma_{i}=t_{i}\end{cases}\\

By the I.H., we have that
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime\prime\prime}:T_{k}
. Then, we can immediately show from
T-Rec
that:

(134)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\forall i \in I : \typesTo{\Gamma}{t_i}{T_i}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'}{\curl{f_i : T_i}_{i \in I}}}

•

T-List

(135)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_0}{T_0} \quad\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_1}{[T_0]}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{\concat{t_0}{t_1}}{[T_0]}}

where
t={t_{0}}\mathbin{\texttt{{:}\!{:}}}{t_{1}}\quad T=[T_{0}]

If both
t_{0}
and
t_{1}
are values, there is no reduction rule, so the premise is false.

Otherwise, we must be able to reduce either
t_{0}
or
t_{1}
further, which can only be done using the context reduction rule
E-
\mathbb{C}
:

(136)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}t'' \reduce{\alpha} t'''\end{gathered}}{\mbC[t''] \reduce{\alpha} \mbC[t''']}

where one of the following assignments apply:

(137)

\displaystyle t^{\prime\prime}=t_{0}\quad\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]=t\quad\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime\prime}]=t^{\prime}\quad\mathbb{C}={\texttt{[\,]}}\mathbin{\texttt{{:}\!{:}}}{t_{1}}

(138)

\displaystyle t^{\prime\prime}=t_{1}\quad\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]=t\quad\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime\prime}]=t^{\prime}\quad\mathbb{C}={t_{0}}\mathbin{\texttt{{:}\!{:}}}{\texttt{[\,]}}

If we use
eq.

137
, we have by the I.H. that
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime\prime\prime}:T_{0}
. Then, we can show by
T-List
that:

(139)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'''}{T_0} \quad\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_1}{[T_0]}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'}{[T_0]}}

Otherwise, if we use
eq.

138
, we have by the I.H. that
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime\prime\prime}:[T_{0}]
.
Then, we can show by
T-List
that:

(140)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_0}{T_0} \quad\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'''}{[T_0]}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'}{[T_0]}}

•

T-Head

(141)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_0}{\listT{T}}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{\listHead{t_0}}{\tx{Option}\ T}}

where
{t=\texttt{head }{t_{0}}}

If
t_{0}
is a value, the only reduction rule that applies is either
E-Head1
or
E-Head2
.

If
E-Head1
was used, we have the following reduction:

(142)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\end{gathered}}{\listHead{\concat{v_0}{v_0'}} \reduce{\tau} \curl{\tx{some} = v_0}}

where
t_{0}={v_{0}}\mathbin{\texttt{{:}\!{:}}}{v_{0}^{\prime}}

Now, from
lemma

B.12
on
\Gamma\vdash t_{0}:[T]
, we have that

{\Gamma\vdash v_{0}:T}
and
{\Gamma\vdash v_{0}^{\prime}:[T]}
.
From
T-Rec
we have that
{\Gamma\vdash\{\text{some}=v_{0}\}:\{\text{some}:T\}}
.
From
ST-Refl
,
ST-
\cup
R
and
T-Sub
, we also have
{\Gamma\vdash\{\text{some}=v_{0}\}:\text{Option}\ T}
,
which gives us the desired result.

If
E-Head2
was used, we have the following reduction:

(143)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\end{gathered}}{\listHead{\nil} \reduce{\tau} \curl{\tx{none} = \unit}}

where
t_{0}=\texttt{nil}

From
T-Val
,
ST-Val
and
Type-Unit
we have
{\Gamma\vdash\texttt{()}:\text{Unit}}
.
From
T-Rec
,
{\Gamma\vdash\{\text{none}=\texttt{()}\}:\{\text{none}:\text{Unit}\}}
.
From
ST-Refl
,
ST-
\cup
R
and
T-Sub
, we have
{\Gamma\vdash\{\text{none}=\texttt{()}\}:\text{Option}\ T}
,
which is the desired result.

Otherwise, if
t_{0}
is not a value, we must be able to reduce it further, which can only be done using the context reduction rule
E-
\mathbb{C}
:

(144)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}t'' \reduce{\alpha} t'''\end{gathered}}{\mbC[t''] \reduce{\alpha} \mbC[t''']}

where
t^{\prime\prime}=t_{0}\quad\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]=t\quad\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime\prime}]=t^{\prime}\quad\mathbb{C}=\texttt{head }{\texttt{[\,]}}

By the I.H.,
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime\prime\prime}:[T]
. Then, we can show by
T-Head
that:

(145)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'''}{[T]}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'}{T}}

•

T-Tail

Analogous to the case for
T-Head
.

T-Let

(146)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_0}{T_0} \quad\typesTo{\Gamma, x : T_0}{t_1}{T_1}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{\letExp{x}{t_0} t_1}{T_1}}

where
t=\texttt{let }x\texttt{ = }t_{0}\texttt{ in }t_{1}\quad T=T_{1}

If
t_{0}
is a value, then the only applicable reduction rule is
E-Let
:

(147)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\end{gathered}}{\letExp{x}{t_0} t_1 \reduce{\tau} \subs{t_1}{x}{t_0}}

From
eq.

146
, we have by
theorem

B.8
that
\Gamma\vdash t_{1}[x\mapsto t_{0}]:T_{1}
,
which is what we want to show.

Otherwise, we must be able to reduce
t_{0}
further, which can only be done using the context reduction rule
E-
\mathbb{C}
:

(148)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}t'' \reduce{\alpha} t'''\end{gathered}}{\mbC[t''] \reduce{\alpha} \mbC[t''']}

where
t^{\prime\prime}=t_{0}\quad\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]=t\quad\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime\prime}]=t^{\prime}\quad\mathbb{C}=\texttt{let }x\texttt{ = }\texttt{[\,]}\texttt{ in }t_{1}

By the I.H.,
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime\prime\prime}:T_{0}
. Then, we can show by
T-Let
that

(149)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'''}{T_0} \quad\typesTo{\Gamma, x : T_0}{t_1}{T_1}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'}{T_1}}

T-App

(150)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_1}{T_1 \rightarrow T_2} \quad\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_2}{T_1}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_1\ t_2}{T_2}}

where
t=t_{1}\ t_{2}\quad T=T_{2}

We first consider the case where both
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
are values.
Then, the only applicable reduction rule is
E-App
:

(151)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\end{gathered}}{(\abs{x}{T_1'} t_0)\ t_2 \reduce{\tau} \subs{t_0}{x}{t_2}}

where
t_{1}=\lambda x:T_{1}^{\prime}.\ t_{0}

From
lemma

B.12
on
{\Gamma\vdash\lambda x:T_{1}^{\prime}.\ t_{0}:T_{1}\rightarrow T_{2}}
we have that

{\Gamma\vdash T_{1}<:T_{1}^{\prime}}
and
{\Gamma,x:T_{1}^{\prime}\vdash t_{0}:T_{2}}
.
First from
T-Sub
we have
{\Gamma\vdash t_{2}:T_{1}^{\prime}}
.
Now by
theorem

B.8
we have that
{\Gamma\vdash t_{0}[x\mapsto t_{2}]:T_{2}}
, which is what we wanted to show.

Otherwise, either
t_{1}
or
t_{2}
must be able to reduce further. Here, we could only have used the context reduction rule
E-
\mathbb{C}
:

(152)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}t'' \reduce{\alpha} t'''\end{gathered}}{\mbC[t''] \reduce{\alpha} \mbC[t''']}

where one of the following assignments applies:

(153)

\displaystyle t^{\prime\prime}=t_{1}\quad\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]=t\quad\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime\prime}]=t^{\prime}\quad\mathbb{C}=\texttt{[\,]}\ t_{2}

(154)

\displaystyle t^{\prime\prime}=t_{2}\quad\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]=t\quad\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime\prime}]=t^{\prime}\quad\mathbb{C}=(\lambda x:T_{1}^{\prime}.\ t_{0})\ \texttt{[\,]}

If
eq.

153
applies, we have by the I.H. that
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime\prime\prime}:T_{1}\rightarrow T_{2}
.
Then, we can show by
T-App
that:

(155)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'''}{T_1 \rightarrow T_2} \quad\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_2}{T_1}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'}{T_2}}

Otherwise, if
eq.

154
applies, we have by the I.H. that
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime\prime\prime}:T_{1}
.
Then, we can again show by
T-App
that:

(156)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'''}{T_1 \rightarrow T_2} \quad\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_2}{T_1}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'''\ t_2}{T_2}}

•

T-TApp

(157)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_1}{\quant{X}{T_1} T_0} \quad\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T_2}{T_1}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_1\ T_2}{\subs{T_0}{X}{T_2}}}

where
t=t_{1}\ T_{2}\quad T=T_{0}[X\mapsto T_{2}]

If
t_{1}
is a value, the only reduction rule that could have been used is
E-TApp
:

(158)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\end{gathered}}{(\tabs{X}{T_1'} t_0)\ T_2 \rightarrow\subs{t_0}{X}{T_2}}

where
t_{1}=\char 12\relax{X}{T_{1}^{\prime}}t_{0}

From
lemma

B.12
on
\Gamma\vdash\char 12\relax{X}{T_{1}^{\prime}}t_{0}:\forall X<:T_{1}.\ T_{0}
we have that

T_{1}=T_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Gamma,X<:T_{1}^{\prime}\vdash t_{0}:T_{0}
.
By
theorem

B.8
, we have
{\Gamma[X\mapsto T_{2}]\vdash t_{0}[X\mapsto T_{2}]:T_{0}[X\mapsto T_{2}]}
.
Since
X
cannot be in
\Gamma
, we have that
\Gamma\vdash t_{0}[X\mapsto T_{2}]:T_{0}[X\mapsto T_{2}]
, which is the desired result.

Otherwise, if
t_{1}
is not a value, it must be able to reduce further. The only applicable rule is
E-
\mathbb{C}
:

(159)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}t'' \reduce{\alpha} t'''\end{gathered}}{\mbC[t''] \reduce{\alpha} \mbC[t''']}

where
t^{\prime\prime}=t_{1}\quad\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]=t\quad\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime\prime}]=t^{\prime}\quad\mathbb{C}=\texttt{[\,]}\ T_{2}

We have by the I.H. that
{\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime\prime\prime}:\forall X<:T_{1}.\ T_{0}}
, then we can show by
T-TApp
that

(160)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'''}{\quant{X}{T_1} T_0} \quad\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T_2}{T_1}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'}{\subs{T_0}{X}{T_2}}}

•

T-Field

(161)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_r}{\curl{f_i : T_i}_{i \in I}} \quad k \in I\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_r.f_k}{T_k}}

where
t=t_{r}.f_{k}\quad T=T_{k}

If
t_{r}
is a value, then the only applicable reduction rule is
E-Field
:

(162)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}t_r = \curl{f_i = v_i}_{i \in I} \quad k \in I\end{gathered}}{t_r.f_k \reduce{\tau} v_k}

From
lemma

B.12
, we have that
\Gamma\vdash v_{k}:T_{k}
, which is what we wanted to show.

Otherwise, if
t_{r}
is not a value, then the only rule that could have been used is
E-
\mathbb{C}
:

(163)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}t'' \reduce{\alpha} t'''\end{gathered}}{\mbC[t''] \reduce{\alpha} \mbC[t''']}

where
t^{\prime\prime}=t_{r}\quad\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]=t\quad\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime\prime}]=t^{\prime}\quad\mathbb{C}=\texttt{[\,]}.f_{k}

By the I.H.,
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime\prime\prime}:\{f_{i}:T_{i}\}_{i\in I}
. Then we have from
T-Field
that

(164)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'''}{\curl{f_i : T_i}_{i \in I}} \quad k \in I\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'}{T_k}}
