T-Match

(165)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_s}{T_s} \quad\forall i \in I: \typesTo{\Gamma, x_i:T_i}{t_i}{T_i'} \quad\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T_s}{\cup_{i \in I} T_i}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{t_s \ttt{ match }\curl{x_i : T_i \Rightarrow t_i}_{i \in I}}{T_s \ttt{ match } \curl{T_i \Rightarrow T_i'}_{i \in I}}}

where
{t=t_{s}\texttt{ match }\{x_{i}:T_{i}\Rightarrow t_{i}\}_{i\in I}\quad T=T_{s}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}}

For convenience, we enumerate each judgement from the typing derivation (omitting the subtyping derivation as it is unnecessary):

(166)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash t_{s}:T_{s}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}

(167)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash t_{s}:T_{s}

(168)

\displaystyle\forall i\in I:(\Gamma,x_{i}:T_{i}\vdash t_{i}:T_{i}^{\prime})

If
t_{s}
is a value
v_{s}
, then the only applicable reduction rule is
E-Match
:

(169)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}k \in I \quad\subtypesTo{\emptyset}{\singleton{v_s}}{T_k} \quad\forall j \in I: j < k \Rightarrow\subtypesTo{\emptyset\not}{\singleton{v_s}}{T_j}\end{gathered}}{v_s \ttt{ match }\curl{x_i:T_i \Rightarrow t_i}_{i \in I} \reduce{\tau} \subs{t_k}{x_k}{v_s}}

First from the premise of
E-Match
, we have that

(170)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash\underline{v_{s}}<:T_{k}

Then, from
lemma

B.10
and
eq.

167
, we have

(171)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash\underline{v_{s}}<:T_{s}

Suppose that
{T^{\prime}=\underline{v_{s}}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}}
.
Now, from
eq.

170
and
lemma

B.2
with the premises of
E-Match
,
we can use
ST-Match1
to conclude

(172)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash T_{k}^{\prime}<:T^{\prime}

We can then use
eq.

171
with
ST-Refl
and
ST-Match2
to conclude

(173)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}<:T_{s}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}

Which means by
ST-Trans
and
eq.

166

(174)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}<:T

Also by
ST-Trans
with
eq.

172
:

(175)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash T_{k}^{\prime}<:T

Now, from
eq.

170
with
T-Val
and
T-Sub
, we have that

(176)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash v_{s}:T_{k}

Then, we can take
k\in I
and
eq.

168
such that
{x_{k}:T_{k}\vdash t_{k}:T_{k}^{\prime}}
. Together with
theorem

B.8
we can show

(177)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash t_{k}[x_{k}\mapsto v_{s}]:T_{k}^{\prime}

By applying
T-Sub
to
eq.

177
and
eq.

175
, we can finally show that

(178)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash t_{k}[x_{k}\mapsto v_{s}]:T

Otherwise, if
t_{s}
is not a value, the only applicable reduction rule is
E-
\mathbb{C}
:

(179)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}t'' \reduce{\alpha} t'''\end{gathered}}{\mbC[t''] \reduce{\alpha} \mbC[t''']}

where
{t^{\prime\prime}=t_{s}\quad\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]=t\quad\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime\prime}]=t^{\prime}\quad\mathbb{C}=\texttt{[\,]}\texttt{ match }\{x_{i}:T_{i}\Rightarrow t_{i}\}_{i\in I}}

By the I.H., we have that
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime\prime\prime}:T_{s}
. Then we have from
T-Match
that:

(180)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'''}{T_s} \quad\forall i \in I: \typesTo{\Gamma, x_i:T_i}{t_i}{T_i'}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{t'}{T_s \ttt{ match } \curl{T_i \Rightarrow T_i'}_{i \in I}}}

T-Sub

(181)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{t}{T'} \quad\subtypesTo{\Gamma}{T'}{T}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{t}{T}}

We can apply the I.H. on
\Gamma\vdash t:T^{\prime}
and get that
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}:T^{\prime}
.
Then, from
T-Sub
, we have
\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}:T
.

T-OpC

(182)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{a}{\tx{ServerRef}[T_m,T_a,T_p]}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{\ttt{Connect}(a)}{\tx{Chan}[T_m,T_a,T_p]}}

t
is not a value, so there must be a reduction. The only applicable rule is

t^{\prime}=s

From
T-Val
,
ST-Val
and
T-Sub
, we have that
{\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}:\text{Chan}[T_{m},T_{a},T_{p}]}
.

,

, we have that
{\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}:\text{Chan}[T_{m},T_{a},T_{p}]}
.

T-OpI

(186)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\Gamma}{s}{\tx{Chan}[T_m,T_a,T_p]} \quad\typesTo{\Gamma}{t}{\PPPPEntity\ T_m\ T_a\ T_p\ X_n\ X_a}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\Gamma}{\ttt{Insert}(s,t)}{\tx{Bool}}}

where
t=\texttt{Insert}(s,t)\quad T=\text{Bool}

t
is not a value, so there must be a reduction. The only applicable rule is
E-Insert
:

(187)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}b \in\tx{Bool}\end{gathered}}{\ttt{Insert}(s,t) \reduceTwo{\tx{insert}(s,t)}{b} b}

where
t^{\prime}=b

From
T-Val
,
ST-Val
and
T-Sub
, we have that
{\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}:\text{Bool}}
.

•

T-OpM
,
T-OpD

Analogous to
T-OpI
.

∎

Theorem B.14 (Type preservation with one client and one server).

If
{\Gamma\vdash t:T}
and
{S|t\rightarrow S^{\prime}|t^{\prime}}
, then
{\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}:T}

Proof.

The proof is by cases on the derivation of
{t|S\rightarrow t^{\prime}|S^{\prime}}
:

•

Net-
\alpha

(188)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}t \reduce{\tau} t'\end{gathered}}{S | t \rightarrow S | t'}

where
S^{\prime}=S

Immediate by applying
lemma

B.13
.

•

Net-Comm

(189)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}S \reduce{\dual{\alpha}} S' \quad t \reduce{\alpha} t'\end{gathered}}{S | t \rightarrow S' | t'}

Immediate by applying
lemma

B.13
.

∎

Proposition B.15 (Network semantics and composition).

Assume
N\xrightarrow{\beta}N^{\prime}
, where
\beta
renges over
\alpha
(from

Definition

5.1
) and
\overline{\alpha}
(from

Definition

5.6
). Then, we have either:

(1)

\beta=\tau
, and either:

•

\exists t,t^{\prime},N_{0}
such that
N\equiv t\mid N_{0}
and
t\xrightarrow{\tau}t^{\prime}
and
N^{\prime}\equiv t^{\prime}\mid N_{0}
, or

•

\exists t,S,t^{\prime},S^{\prime},N_{0}
such that
N\equiv t\mid S\mid N_{0}
and
t\mid S\xrightarrow{\tau}t^{\prime}\mid S^{\prime}
and
N^{\prime}\equiv t^{\prime}\mid S^{\prime}\mid N_{0}
;

(2)

\beta\neq\tau
, and either:

•

\exists t,t^{\prime},N_{0}
such that
N\equiv t\mid N_{0}
and
t\xrightarrow{\beta}t^{\prime}
and
N^{\prime}\equiv t^{\prime}\mid N_{0}
, or

•

\exists S,S^{\prime},N_{0}
such that
N\equiv S\mid N_{0}
and
S\xrightarrow{\beta}S^{\prime}
and
N^{\prime}\equiv t^{\prime}\mid S^{\prime}\mid N_{0}
;

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of the transition
N\xrightarrow{\alpha}N^{\prime}
,
according to
Definition

5.7
, and by

Definition

6.2
.

∎

Corollary B.16 (Type preservation (one step)).

If
{\Gamma\vdash t:T}
and
t\mid N\xrightarrow{}t^{\prime}\mid N^{\prime}
, then
{\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}:T}
.

Proof.

Direct consequence of
Theorem

B.14
and

Proposition

B.15
.

∎

See
6.1

Proof.

By hypothesis we have, for some
n\geq 0
:

t\mid N=N_{0}\xrightarrow{}N_{1}\xrightarrow{}\cdots\xrightarrow{}N_{n}=t^{\prime}\mid N^{\prime}

We prove the thesis by induction on
n
.

•

Base case
n=0
. We have
t=t^{\prime}
, hence the statement holds trivially;

•

Inductive case
n=m+1
. We have
t\mid N=N_{0}\xrightarrow{}N_{1}\xrightarrow{}\cdots\xrightarrow{}N_{m}=t^{\prime\prime}\mid N^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{}t^{\prime}\mid N^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis, we have
{\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime\prime}:T}
.
Therefore, by
Corollary

B.16
, we conclude
{\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}:T}
.

∎

Lemma B.17 (Channel consistency).

If
{\emptyset\vdash t\ \text{type}}
,
\emptyset\vdash t:T
and
t\xrightarrow{\alpha}t^{\prime}

and
\forall a\in t:\exists T^{a}_{m},T^{a}_{a},T^{a}_{p}:a\in\text{ServerRef}[T^{a}_{m},T^{a}_{a},T^{a}_{p}]

and
\forall s\in t:\exists T^{s}_{m},T^{s}_{a},T^{s}_{p}:s\in\text{Chan}[T^{s}_{m},T^{s}_{a},T^{s}_{p}]

Then
\alpha
must be one of the following labels:

\displaystyle\cdot\

\displaystyle\alpha="\tau"

\displaystyle\text{where }\forall a:a\in t^{\prime}\Rightarrow a\in t\wedge\forall s:s\in t^{\prime}\Rightarrow s\in t

\displaystyle\cdot\

\displaystyle\alpha="\text{connect}(a^{\prime})=s^{\prime}"

\displaystyle\text{where }\forall a\in t^{\prime}:a\in t\wedge\forall s\in t^{\prime}:(s\in t\vee s=s^{\prime})\wedge s^{\prime}\in\text{Chan}[T^{a^{\prime}}_{m},T^{a^{\prime}}_{a},T^{a^{\prime}}_{p}]

\displaystyle\cdot\

\displaystyle\alpha=("\text{read}(s^{\prime},v)=r"\ |\ "\text{insert}(s^{\prime},v)=b"\ |\ "\text{update}(s^{\prime},v)=b"\ |\ "\text{delete}(s^{\prime},v)=b")

\displaystyle\text{where }\exists X_{n},X_{a}:\emptyset\vdash v:\text{P4Entity}\ T^{s^{\prime}}_{m}\ T^{s^{\prime}}_{a}\ T^{s^{\prime}}_{p}\ X_{n}\ X_{a}

\displaystyle\text{and }s^{\prime}\in t\wedge\forall a:a\in t\Leftrightarrow a\in t^{\prime}\wedge\forall s\in t^{\prime}:s\in t

Proof.

The proof is by induction on the derivation of
t\xrightarrow{\alpha}t^{\prime}
. We consider each case:

•

E-App

(190)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\end{gathered}}{(\lambda x : T_1 . t_0) v \reduce{\tau} \subs{t_0}{x}{v}}

where
t=(\lambda x:T_{1}.t_{0})v\quad t^{\prime}=t_{0}[x\mapsto v]\quad\alpha=\tau

t_{0}
contains a subset of the values in
t
, in fact, the only possible change is
v
subsituting some variable. As such, we have that:

(191)

\displaystyle\forall a:a\in t_{0}\cup\{v\}\Leftrightarrow a\in t

(192)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\forall s:s\in t_{0}\cup\{v\}\Leftrightarrow s\in t

The substitution
t_{0}[x\mapsto v]
only replaces free variables; any existing values are unchanged from
t_{0}
.
However, if there are no free variables
x
in
t_{0}
,
t_{0}[x\mapsto v]
will not (necessarily) contain any
v
.
We can then conclude:

(193)

\displaystyle\forall a:a\in t_{0}[x\mapsto v]\Rightarrow a\in t_{0}\cup\{v\}

(194)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\forall s:s\in t_{0}[x\mapsto v]\Rightarrow s\in t_{0}\cup\{v\}

From this we have:

(195)

\forall a:a\in t^{\prime}\Rightarrow a\in t\wedge\forall s:s\in t^{\prime}\Rightarrow s\in t

which is what we want to show for
\alpha=\tau
.

•

E-TApp

(196)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\end{gathered}}{(\lambda X<:T_1. t_0)\ T_2 \reduce{\tau} \subs{t_0}{X}{T_2}}

where
t=(\lambda X<:T_{1}.t_{0})\ T_{2}\quad t^{\prime}=t_{0}[X\mapsto T_{2}]\quad\alpha=\tau

By the structure of
t
, we know that

(197)

\displaystyle\forall a:a\in t_{0}\Leftrightarrow a\in t

(198)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\forall s:s\in t_{0}\Leftrightarrow s\in t

Type substitution does not change the values in
t_{0}
, so all values remain unchanged:

(199)

\displaystyle\forall a:a\in t_{0}[X\mapsto T_{1}]\Leftrightarrow a\in t_{0}

(200)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\forall s:s\in t_{0}[X\mapsto T_{1}]\Leftrightarrow s\in t_{0}

Now we can directly show that:

(201)

\displaystyle\forall a:a\in t\Leftrightarrow a\in t^{\prime}

(202)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\forall s:s\in t\Leftrightarrow s\in t^{\prime}

from which we obtain the conclusion we want to show for
\alpha=\tau
.

•

E-Let

(203)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\end{gathered}}{\letExp{x}{v} t_0 \reduce{\tau} \subs{t_0}{x}{v}}

where
t=\texttt{let }x\texttt{ = }v\texttt{ in }t\quad t[x\mapsto v]\quad\alpha=\tau

This case is analogous to the one for
E-App
.

•

E-Field

(204)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}v = \curl{f_i=v_i}_{i \in I} \quad k \in I\end{gathered}}{v.f_k \reduce{\tau} v_k}

where
t=v.f_{k}\quad t^{\prime}=v_{k}\quad\alpha=\tau

Since
v_{k}
is the value of one of the fields in
v
, and
v
may have more fields, we have that:

(205)

\displaystyle\forall a:a\in v_{k}\Rightarrow a\in v

(206)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\forall s:s\in v_{k}\Rightarrow s\in v

Which gives us what we want to show.

•

E-Head

(207)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\end{gathered}}{\listHead{\concat{v_0}{v_1}} \reduce{\tau} v_0}

where
t=\texttt{head }{{v_{0}}\mathbin{\texttt{{:}\!{:}}}{v_{1}}}\quad t^{\prime}=v_{0}\quad\alpha=\tau

Since the entire list
{v_{0}}\mathbin{\texttt{{:}\!{:}}}{v_{1}}
must have at least as many values as
v_{0}
, we have:

(208)

\displaystyle\forall a:a\in v_{0}\Rightarrow a\in{v_{0}}\mathbin{\texttt{{:}\!{:}}}{v_{1}}

(209)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\forall s:s\in v_{0}\Rightarrow s\in{v_{0}}\mathbin{\texttt{{:}\!{:}}}{v_{1}}

Which is what we want to show.

•

E-Tail

(210)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\end{gathered}}{\listTail{\concat{v_0}{v_1}} \reduce{\tau} v_1}

where
t=\texttt{tail }{{v_{0}}\mathbin{\texttt{{:}\!{:}}}{v_{1}}}\quad t^{\prime}=v_{1}\quad\alpha=\tau

Same argument as for
E-Head
, but with
t^{\prime}=v_{1}
instead.

•

E-Match

(211)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}k \in I \quad\subtypesTo{\emptyset}{\singleton{v}}{T_k} \quad\forall j \in I: j < k \Rightarrow\subtypesTo{\emptyset\not}{\singleton{v}}{T_j}\end{gathered}}{v \ttt{ match }\curl{x_i:T_i \Rightarrow t_i}_{i \in I} \reduce{\tau} \subs{t_k}{x_k}{v}}

where
t=v\texttt{ match }\{x_{i}:T_{i}\Rightarrow t_{i}\}_{i\in I}\quad t^{\prime}=t_{k}[x_{k}\mapsto v]\quad\alpha=\tau

t_{k}
is only one of the branches of
t
,
and the substitution
t_{k}[x_{k}\mapsto v]
does not introduce any new variables,
so we immediately have

(212)

\displaystyle\forall a:a\in t_{k}[x_{k}\mapsto v]\Rightarrow a\in t

(213)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\forall s:s\in t_{k}[x_{k}\mapsto v]\Rightarrow s\in t

Which is what we wanted to show.

•

E-Connect

(214)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}a' \in\tx{ServerRef}[T_m, T_a, T_p] \quad s' \in\tx{Chan}[T_m, T_a, T_p]\end{gathered}}{\ttt{Connect}(a') \reduce{\tx{connect}(a')\ =\ s'} s'}

where
t=\texttt{Connect}(a^{\prime})\quad t^{\prime}=s^{\prime}\quad\alpha="\text{connect}(a^{\prime})=s^{\prime}"

We want to make the following conclusion:

(215)

\displaystyle\forall a\in t^{\prime}:a\in t

(216)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\forall s\in t^{\prime}:(s\in t\vee s=s^{\prime})

(217)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle s^{\prime}\in\text{Chan}[T^{a^{\prime}}_{m},T^{a^{\prime}}_{a},T^{a^{\prime}}_{p}]

Equation

215
holds vacuously, as there is no
a
in
t^{\prime}
.

Equation

216
holds, as the only
s
in
t^{\prime}
is
s^{\prime}
.

Now, from the premise of the lemma, we have that

(218)

\forall a\in t:\exists T^{a}_{m},T^{a}_{a},T^{a}_{p}:a\in\text{ServerRef}[T^{a}_{m},T^{a}_{a},T^{a}_{p}]

Since
s^{\prime}
uses the same internal channel types as
a^{\prime}
, we must have (from the premises of
E-Connect
)
that
s^{\prime}\in\text{Chan}[T^{a^{\prime}}_{m},T^{a^{\prime}}_{a},T^{a^{\prime}}_{p}]
, which shows that
eq.

217
holds.

•

E-Read

(219)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}s' \in\tx{Chan}[T_m,T_a,T_p] \quad v \in\PPPPEntity\ T_m\ T_a\ T_p\ X_n\ X_a\end{gathered}}{\ttt{Read}(s',v) \reduce{\tx{read}(s',v)\ =\ r} r}

where
t=\texttt{Read}(s^{\prime},v)\quad t^{\prime}=r\quad{\alpha="\text{read}(s^{\prime},v)\ =\ r"}

We want to make the following conclusion:

(220)

\displaystyle\emptyset\vdash v:\text{P4Entity}\ T^{s^{\prime}}_{m}\ T^{s^{\prime}}_{a}\ T^{s^{\prime}}_{p}\ X_{n}\ X_{a}

(221)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle s^{\prime}\in t

(222)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\forall a:a\in t\Leftrightarrow a\in t^{\prime}

(223)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\forall s\in t^{\prime}:s\in t

Equation

221
is immediate, as
s^{\prime}\in\texttt{Read}(s^{\prime},v)
.

From
T-Val
,
ST-Val
and
T-Sub
we have that

(224)

\displaystyle\emptyset\vdash s^{\prime}:\text{Chan}[T_{m},T_{a},T_{p}]

(225)

\displaystyle\emptyset\vdash v:\text{P4Entity}\ T^{s^{\prime}}_{m}\ T^{s^{\prime}}_{a}\ T^{s^{\prime}}_{p}\ X_{n}\ X_{a}

Now, from the premise of the lemma, we have that

(226)

\forall s\in t:\exists T^{s}_{m},T^{s}_{a},T^{s}_{p}:s\in\text{Chan}[T^{s}_{m},T^{s}_{a},T^{s}_{p}]

So from
eq.

224
it must be the case that

(227)

\emptyset\vdash s^{\prime}:\text{Chan}[T^{s^{\prime}}_{m},T^{s^{\prime}}_{a},T^{s^{\prime}}_{p}]

Equation

220
is identical to
eq.

225
.

Since there are no
a
in
t
or
t^{\prime}
,
eq.

222
holds vacuously.

Also, since there are no
s
in
t^{\prime}
,
eq.

223
also holds vacuously.

•

E-Insert

(228)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\end{gathered}}{\ttt{Insert}(s',v) \reduce{\tx{insert}(s',v)\ =\ b} b}

where
t=\texttt{Insert}(s^{\prime},v)\quad t^{\prime}=b\quad{\alpha="\text{insert}(s^{\prime},v)\ =\ b"}

This case is analogous to the one for
E-Read
.

•

E-Modify

(229)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\end{gathered}}{\ttt{Modify}(s',v) \reduce{\tx{modify}(s',v)\ =\ b} b}

where
t=\texttt{Modify}(s^{\prime},v)\quad t^{\prime}=b\quad{\alpha="\text{modify}(s^{\prime},v)\ =\ b"}

This case is analogous to the one for
E-Read
.

•

E-Delete

(230)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\end{gathered}}{\ttt{Delete}(s',v) \reduce{\tx{delete}(s',v)\ =\ b} b}

where
t=\texttt{Delete}(s^{\prime},v)\quad t^{\prime}=b\quad{\alpha="\text{delete}(s^{\prime},v)\ =\ b"}

This case is analogous to the one for
E-Read
.

•

E-
\mathbb{C}

(231)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}t'' \reduce{\alpha} t'''\end{gathered}}{\mbC[t''] \reduce{\alpha} \mbC[t''']}

where
t=\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]\quad t^{\prime}=\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime\prime}]

Since the context
\mathbb{C}
surrounding
t^{\prime\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
is the same before and after reduction, we know that all
a
and
s
in
\mathbb{C}
are unchanged. Formally,

(232)

\displaystyle\forall s:s\in\mathbb{C}\Rightarrow s\in\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]\wedge s\in\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime\prime}]

(233)

\displaystyle\forall a:a\in\mathbb{C}\Rightarrow a\in\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]\wedge a\in\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime\prime}]

By the I.H. on the derivation of
{t^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}t^{\prime\prime\prime}}
, we know that
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
satisfies the properties determined by
\alpha
.
We consider each case of
\alpha

–

\alpha="\tau"

By the I.H. on
t^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}t^{\prime\prime\prime}
:

(234)

\displaystyle\forall s:s\in t^{\prime\prime\prime}\Rightarrow s\in t^{\prime\prime}

(235)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\forall a:a\in t^{\prime\prime\prime}\Rightarrow a\in t^{\prime\prime}

From
eq.

234
and
eq.

232
we get

(236)

\forall s:s\in\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime\prime}]\Rightarrow s\in\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]

And likewise with
eq.

235
and
eq.

233
:

(237)

\forall a:a\in\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime\prime}]\Rightarrow a\in\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]

which is what we wanted to show.

–

\alpha="\text{connect}(a)=s^{\prime}"

By the I.H. on
t^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}t^{\prime\prime\prime}
:

(238)

\displaystyle\forall s\in t^{\prime\prime\prime}:(s\in t^{\prime\prime}\vee s=s^{\prime})

(239)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle s^{\prime}\in\text{Chan}[T^{a}_{m},T^{a}_{a},T^{a}_{p}]

(240)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\forall a\in t^{\prime\prime\prime}:a\in t^{\prime\prime}

From
eq.

238
and
eq.

232
, we have

(241)

\forall s\in\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime\prime}]:(s\in\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]\vee s=s^{\prime})

Equation

239
is exactly the same as the conclusion we want to show.

From
eq.

240
and
eq.

233
, we have

(242)

\forall a\in\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime\prime}]:a\in\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]

which concludes this case.

–

{\alpha=("\text{read}(s^{\prime},v)=r"\ |\ "\text{insert}(s,v)=b"\ |\ "\text{update}(s^{\prime},v)=b"\ |\ "\text{delete}(s^{\prime},v)=b")}

By the I.H. on
t^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\alpha}t^{\prime\prime\prime}
:

(243)

\displaystyle\emptyset\vdash v:\text{P4Entity}\ T^{s^{\prime}}_{m}\ T^{s^{\prime}}_{a}\ T^{s^{\prime}}_{p}\ X_{n}\ X_{a}

(244)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle s^{\prime}\in t^{\prime\prime}

(245)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\forall a:a\in t^{\prime\prime}\Leftrightarrow a\in t^{\prime\prime\prime}

(246)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\forall s\in t^{\prime\prime\prime}:s\in t^{\prime\prime}

Clearly, if
s^{\prime}\in t^{\prime\prime}
from
eq.

244
then
s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]
.

Equation

243
is the same as the desired conclusion.

From
eq.

245
and
eq.

233
, we have

(247)

\forall a:a\in\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]\Leftrightarrow a\in\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime\prime}]

From
eq.

246
and
eq.

232
, we have

(248)

\forall s\in\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime\prime}]:s\in\mathbb{C}[t^{\prime\prime}]

∎

Theorem B.18 (Term progress).

If
{\emptyset\vdash t\ \text{type}}
and
{\emptyset\vdash t:T}

and
\forall a\in t:\exists T^{a}_{m},T^{a}_{a},T^{a}_{p}:a\in\text{ServerRef}[T^{a}_{m},T^{a}_{a},T^{a}_{p}]

and
\forall s\in t:\exists T^{s}_{m},T^{s}_{a},T^{s}_{p}:s\in\text{Chan}[T^{s}_{m},T^{s}_{a},T^{s}_{p}]

Then, either
t
is a value or there is a reduction
t\xrightarrow{\alpha}t^{\prime}
such that:

\displaystyle\cdot\

\displaystyle\alpha="\tau"

\displaystyle\text{where }\forall a:a\in t^{\prime}\Rightarrow a\in t\wedge\forall s:s\in t^{\prime}\Rightarrow s\in t

\displaystyle\cdot\

\displaystyle\alpha="\text{connect}(a^{\prime})=s^{\prime}"

\displaystyle\text{where }\forall a\in t^{\prime}:a\in t\wedge\forall s\in t^{\prime}:(s\in t\vee s=s^{\prime})\wedge s^{\prime}\in\text{Chan}[T^{a^{\prime}}_{m},T^{a^{\prime}}_{a},T^{a^{\prime}}_{p}]

\displaystyle\cdot\

\displaystyle\alpha=("\text{read}(s^{\prime},v)=r"\ |\ "\text{insert}(s^{\prime},v)=b"\ |\ "\text{update}(s^{\prime},v)=b"\ |\ "\text{delete}(s^{\prime},v)=b")

\displaystyle\text{where }\emptyset\vdash v:\text{P4Entity}\ T^{s^{\prime}}_{m}\ T^{s^{\prime}}_{a}\ T^{s^{\prime}}_{p}\ X_{n}\ X_{a}

\displaystyle\text{and }s^{\prime}\in t\wedge\forall a:a\in t\Leftrightarrow a\in t^{\prime}\wedge\forall s\in t^{\prime}:s\in t

Proof.

We show the theorem by a proof by induction on the derivation of
\emptyset\vdash t:T
.

•

T-Val

where
t=v\quad T=\underline{v}

t
is always a value, so the conclusion is immediate.

•

T-Rec

where
t=\{f_{i}=t_{i}\}_{i\in I}\quad T=\{f_{i}:T_{i}\}_{i\in I}

By the I.H., all
t_{i}
are either values or reduce to some
t_{i}^{\prime}
.

If all of them are values,
t
is also a value by definition, and so we have the conclusion.

If at least one of them, say
t_{k}
where
k\in I
, is not a value, then by the I.H. there is a
reduction
t_{k}\xrightarrow{\alpha}t_{k}^{\prime}
.
We can then apply the context rule
E-
\mathbb{C}
to get a reduction

(251)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}t_k \reduce{\alpha} t_k'\end{gathered}}{\mbC[t_k] \reduce{\alpha} \mbC[t_k']}

where
t=\mathbb{C}[t_{k}],t^{\prime}=\mathbb{C}[t_{k}^{\prime}]
and

(252)

\mathbb{C}\ =\ \{f_{i}=\gamma_{i}\}_{i\in I}\text{ where }\forall i\in I:\begin{cases}i<k\Rightarrow\gamma_{i}=v_{i}\\
i=k\Rightarrow\gamma_{i}=\texttt{[\,]}\\
i>k\Rightarrow\gamma_{i}=t_{i}\end{cases}

Since we have a reduction, we can use
lemma

B.17
to obtain the desired conclusion.

•

T-List

(253)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\emptyset}{t_0}{T_0} \quad\typesTo{\emptyset}{t_1}{[T_0]}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\emptyset}{\concat{t_0}{t_1}}{[T_0]}}

where
t={t_{0}}\mathbin{\texttt{{:}\!{:}}}{t_{1}}\quad T=[T_{0}]

By the I.H.,
t_{0}
and
t_{1}
are either values or there exists reductions

t_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}t_{0}^{\prime}
and
t_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha}t_{1}^{\prime}
respectively.
If
t_{0}
is not a value, we can apply
E-
\mathbb{C}
to get a reduction of
t=\mathbb{C}[t_{0}]
, where:

(254)

\mathbb{C}\ =\ {\texttt{[\,]}}\mathbin{\texttt{{:}\!{:}}}{t_{1}}

If
t_{0}
is a value and
t_{1}
is not, we can again use the context rule
E-
\mathbb{C}
to get a reduction of
t=\mathbb{C}[t_{1}]
, where:

(255)

\mathbb{C}\ =\ {t_{0}}\mathbin{\texttt{{:}\!{:}}}{\texttt{[\,]}}

In any reduction case, we can use
lemma

B.17
to obtain the desired result.

If both are values,
t
is also a value, and so we again have the desired result.

•

T-Head

(256)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\emptyset}{t_0}{[T_0]}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\emptyset}{\listHead{t_0}}{T_0}}

where
t=\texttt{head }{t_{0}}\quad T=T_{0}

By the I.H., either
t_{0}
is a value or there exists a reduction
t_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}t_{0}^{\prime}
.
If
t_{0}
is not a value, we can apply
E-
\mathbb{C}
to get a reduction of
t=\mathbb{C}[t_{0}]
, where:

(257)

\mathbb{C}=\texttt{head }{\texttt{[\,]}}

Otherwise, we can use
E-Head
to obtain a reduction.
In both reduction cases, we apply
lemma

B.17
to obtain the desired result.

•

T-Tail

Analogous to the case for
T-Head
.

•

T-Var

where
t=x

Vacuously holds, as
x:T\not\in\emptyset
.

•

T-Let

where
t=\texttt{let }x\texttt{ = }t_{0}\texttt{ in }t_{1}\quad T=T_{1}

By the I.H., either
t_{0}
is a value or reduces to some
t_{0}^{\prime}
.

If
t_{0}
is not a value, there is a reduction
t_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}t_{0}^{\prime}
.
We can apply
E-
\mathbb{C}
to get a reduction of
t=\mathbb{C}[t_{0}]
, where

(260)

\mathbb{C}\ =\ \texttt{let }x\texttt{ = }\texttt{[\,]}\texttt{ in }t_{1}

Otherwise, if
t_{0}
is a value,
t
can be reduced with the
E-Let
rule.

Since
t
can always be reduced, we can apply
lemma

B.17
to obtain the conclusion.

•

T-Abs

where
t=\lambda x:T_{1}.t_{2}\quad T=T_{1}\rightarrow T_{2}

t
is a value, so the conclusion is immediate.

•

T-App

where
t=t_{1}\ t_{2}\quad T=T_{2}

By the I.H., either
t_{1}
is a value or reduces to some
t_{1}^{\prime}
.

If
t_{1}
is not a value, we can apply
E-
\mathbb{C}
to get a reduction of
t=\mathbb{C}[t_{1}]
, where

(263)

\mathbb{C}\ =\ \texttt{[\,]}\ t_{2}

If
t_{1}
is a value, it must have the form
v_{1}=\lambda x:T_{1}^{\prime}.t_{0}
,
where
{\emptyset\vdash T_{1}^{\prime}<:T_{1}}
.
Then, by the I.H., either
t_{2}
is a value or it reduces to some
t_{2}^{\prime}
.

If
t_{2}
is not a value, we can again apply
E-
\mathbb{C}
to get a reduction of
t=\mathbb{C}[t_{2}]
, where

(264)

\mathbb{C}\ =\ v_{1}\ \texttt{[\,]}

If
t_{2}
is a value,
t
can be reduced with the
E-App
rule.

Since
t
can always be reduced, we can apply
lemma

B.17
to obtain the conclusion.

•

T-TAbs

where
t=\lambda X<:T_{1}.t_{2}\quad T=\forall X<:T_{1}.T_{2}

t
is a value, so the conclusion is immediate.

•

T-TApp

where
t=t_{1}\ T_{2}\quad T=T_{0}[X\mapsto T_{1}^{\prime}]

By the I.H., either
t_{1}
is a value or reduces to some
t_{1}^{\prime}
.

If
t_{1}
is not a value, we can apply
E-
\mathbb{C}
to get a reduction of
t=\mathbb{C}[t_{1}]
, where

(267)

\mathbb{C}\ =\ \texttt{[\,]}\ T_{2}

If
t_{1}
is a value, it must have the form
v_{1}=\lambda X<:T_{1}^{\prime}.t_{0}
,
where
{\emptyset\vdash T_{1}<:T_{1}^{\prime}}
.
Then
t
can be reduced with the
E-TApp
rule.

Since
t
can always be reduced, we can apply
lemma

B.17
to get the conclusion.

•

T-Field

where
t=t_{r}.f_{k}\quad T_{k}

By the I.H., either
t_{r}
is a value (all fields are values) or it reduces to some
t_{r}^{\prime}

(at least one field is not a value).

If
t_{r}
is not a value, we can apply
E-
\mathbb{C}
to get a reduction of
t=\mathbb{C}[t_{r}]
, where

(269)

\mathbb{C}\ =\ \texttt{[\,]}.f_{k}

If
t_{0}
is a value,
t
can be reduced with the
E-Field
rule.

Since
t
can always be reduced, we can apply
lemma

B.17
to obtain the conclusion.

•

T-Match

where
t=t_{s}\texttt{ match }\{x_{i}:T_{i}\Rightarrow t_{i}\}_{i\in I}\quad T=T_{s}\texttt{ match }\{T_{i}\Rightarrow T_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}

By the I.H., either
t_{s}
is a value or it reduces to some
t_{s}^{\prime}
.

If
t_{s}
is not a value, we can apply
E-
\mathbb{C}
to get a reduction of
t=\mathbb{C}[t_{s}]
, where

(271)

\mathbb{C}\ =\ \texttt{[\,]}\texttt{ match }\{x_{i}:T_{i}\Rightarrow t_{i}\}_{i\in I}

If
t_{s}
is a value (
v_{s}
), the only other rule that could potentially be applied is
E-Match
.
We now show that this rule
can
in fact be applied.

First, from
lemma

B.10
and we have that

(272)

\emptyset\vdash\underline{v_{s}}<:T_{s}

From the premises and
ST-Trans
:

(273)

\emptyset\vdash\underline{v_{s}}<:\cup_{i\in I}T_{i}

The subtyping derivation of
eq.

273
must have used
ST-
\cup
R
,
so we must be able to go up the derivation tree and find at least one of the unioned types which alone is a supertype of
\underline{v_{s}}
.
We pick the supertype
T_{k}
such that there is no
j<k,j\in I
for which
T_{j}
is a supertype of
T_{s}
. Now, we have that

(274)

\emptyset\vdash\underline{v_{s}}<:T_{k}

and

(275)

\displaystyle\forall j\in I:j<k\Rightarrow\emptyset\not\vdash\underline{v_{s}}<:T_{j}

Now, we can apply
E-Match
to achieve the reduction.

Since we have now shown that
t
can always be reduced, we can apply
lemma

B.17
to obtain the desired conclusion.

•

T-Sub

Since the derivation tree is finite, we can apply the I.H. to the derivation
\emptyset\vdash t:T^{\prime}
.
This tells us that either
t
is a value or it can be reduced to some
t^{\prime}
, which is what we want to show.

•

T-OpC

where
t=\texttt{Connect}(a)\quad T=\text{Chan}[T_{m},T_{a},T_{p}]

By definition,
a
must be a value (or a variable, but this would immediately make the premise false).

\texttt{Connect}(a)
is not a value, so there must be a reduction to some
t^{\prime}
.
Since
{\emptyset\vdash a:\text{ServerRef}[T_{m},T_{a},T_{p}]}
, we have
{a\in\text{ServerRef}[T_{m},T_{a},T_{p}]}
by definition.
Also, from
lemma

B.13
, we have that
{\emptyset\vdash t^{\prime}:\text{Chan}[T_{m},T_{a},T_{p}]}
, which again gives us that
{t^{\prime}\in\text{Chan}[T_{m},T_{a},T_{p}]}
.
Now, we can use
E-Connect
for the reduction
t\xrightarrow{\text{connect}(a)\ =\ s}t^{\prime}
, which allows us to use
lemma

B.17
to get the conclusion.

•

T-OpR

(278)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\emptyset}{s}{\tx{Chan}[T_m,T_a,T_p]} \quad\typesTo{\emptyset}{v}{\PPPPEntity\ T_m\ T_a\ T_p\ X_n\ X_a}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\emptyset}{\ttt{Read}(s,v)}{[\PPPPEntity\ T_m\ T_a\ T_p\ X_n\ X_a]}}

where
t=\texttt{Read}(s,v)\quad T=[\text{P4Entity}\ T_{m}\ T_{a}\ T_{p}\ X_{n}\ X_{a}]

By definition,
s
and
v
are both values, but
\texttt{Read}(s,v)
is not a value, so there must be a reduction to some
t^{\prime}
.
Since
\emptyset\vdash s:\text{Chan}[T_{m},T_{a},T_{p}]
, we have
{s\in\text{Chan}[T_{m},T_{a},T_{p}]}
by definition.

The same applies for
{\emptyset\vdash v:\text{P4Entity}\ T_{m}\ T_{a}\ T_{p}\ X_{n}\ X_{a}}
and

{v\in\text{P4Entity}\ T_{m}\ T_{a}\ T_{p}\ X_{n}\ X_{a}}
.
Now, the only applicable reduction is
E-Read
, which gives us the reduction
{t\xrightarrow{{\text{read}(s,v)}\leadsto{r}}}
.
We then use
lemma

B.17
to get the desired result.

•

T-OpI

(279)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}\typesTo{\emptyset}{s}{\tx{Chan}[T_m,T_a,T_p]} \quad\typesTo{\emptyset}{v}{\PPPPEntity\ T_m\ T_a\ T_p\ X_n\ X_a}\end{gathered}}{\typesTo{\emptyset}{\ttt{Insert}(s,v)}{\tx{Bool}}}

where
t=\texttt{Insert}(s,v)\quad T=\text{Unit}

By definition, all of the arguments must be values (otherwise the premise is false).

\texttt{Insert}(s,v)
is not a value, so there must be a reduction to some
t^{\prime}
.
The only applicable reduction rule is
E-Insert
, so we use it to obtain the reduction
{t\xrightarrow{\text{insert}(s,v)\ =\ \texttt{()}}t^{\prime}}
.
We then use
lemma

B.17
to get the conclusion.

•

T-OpM

where
t=\texttt{Modify}(s,v)\quad T=\text{Unit}

This case is analogous to the one for
T-OpI
.

•

T-OpD

where
t=\texttt{Delete}(s,v)\quad T=\text{Unit}

This case is analogous to the one for
T-OpI
.

∎

In order to reduce verbosity, we define:

\displaystyle C.\mathit{table\_matches}

\displaystyle=C.\mathit{tm}

\displaystyle C.\mathit{table\_actions}

\displaystyle=C.\mathit{ta}

\displaystyle C.\mathit{action\_params}

\displaystyle=C.\mathit{ap}

Theorem B.19 (Progress (with explicit invariants)).

Assume
\emptyset\vdash t:T
and
\lx@sectionsign=\langle C,E,a^{\prime},K\rangle

and
\forall a\in t:a=a^{\prime}\wedge a\in\text{ServerRef}[\llbracket C.\mathit{tm}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.\mathit{ta}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.\mathit{ap}\rrbracket]

and
\forall s\in t:s\in K\wedge s\in\text{Chan}[\llbracket C.\mathit{tm}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.\mathit{ta}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.\mathit{ap}\rrbracket]

Then, either
t
is a value or
t|S\rightarrow t^{\prime}|S^{\prime}
where
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}=\langle C^{\prime},E^{\prime},a^{\prime\prime},K^{\prime}\rangle

and
\forall a\in t^{\prime}:a=a^{\prime\prime}\wedge a\in\text{ServerRef}[\llbracket C.\mathit{tm}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.\mathit{ta}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.\mathit{ap}\rrbracket]

and
\forall s\in t^{\prime}:s\in K^{\prime}\wedge s\in\text{Chan}[\llbracket C.\mathit{tm}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.\mathit{ta}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.\mathit{ap}\rrbracket]

Proof.

If we take

\displaystyle T^{a}_{m},T^{s}_{m}

\displaystyle=\llbracket C.\mathit{tm}\rrbracket

(282)

\displaystyle T^{a}_{a},T^{s}_{a}

\displaystyle=\llbracket C.\mathit{ta}\rrbracket

\displaystyle T^{a}_{p},T^{s}_{p}

\displaystyle=\llbracket C.\mathit{ap}\rrbracket

Then we have that

(283)

\displaystyle\forall a\in t:\exists T^{a}_{m},T^{a}_{a},T^{a}_{p}:a\in\text{ServerRef}[T^{a}_{m},T^{a}_{a},T^{a}_{p}]

(284)

\displaystyle\forall s\in t:\exists T^{s}_{m},T^{s}_{a},T^{s}_{p}:s\in\text{Chan}[T^{s}_{m},T^{s}_{a},T^{s}_{p}]

Which means that we can use
theorem

B.18
to show that either
t
is a value or there is a reduction
{t\xrightarrow{\alpha}t^{\prime}}
.

If
t
is a value, we are done. Otherwise, we must show that there exists a reduction
{t|S\rightarrow t^{\prime}|S^{\prime}}
.
The proof is by cases on the
\alpha
label of the
t
reduction:

•

\alpha="\tau"

where

(285)

\forall a:a\in t^{\prime}\Rightarrow a\in t\wedge\forall s:s\in t^{\prime}\Rightarrow s\in t

Since the server cannot reduce with label
\overline{\tau}
by any rule,
the reduction must have used the
Net-
\alpha
rule with an
internal
\tau
-transition
t\xrightarrow{\tau}t^{\prime}
of the client,
hence we have
S=S^{\prime}
.
From
eq.

285
,
t^{\prime}
does not gain any new
a
or
s
,
so the properties in the premise which hold for
t
must also hold for
t^{\prime}
in the conclusion.

•

\alpha="\text{connect}(a^{\prime})=s^{\prime}"

where

(286)

\displaystyle\forall a\in t^{\prime}:a\in t

(287)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\forall s\in t^{\prime}:(s\in t\vee s=s^{\prime})

(288)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle s^{\prime}\in\text{Chan}[T^{a^{\prime}}_{m},T^{a^{\prime}}_{a},T^{a^{\prime}}_{p}]

The only rule that can reduce the server with label
\overline{\text{connect}(a^{\prime})=s^{\prime}}
is
Sv-Connect
:

(289)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}s' \tx{ is a fresh variable} \quad s' \in\tx{Chan}[\encoded{\tm{C}}, \encoded{\ta{C}}, \encoded{\ap{C}}]\end{gathered}}{\serverRed{C, E, a', K}{\tx{connect}(a')}{s'}{C, E, a', K \cup\curl{s'}}}

such that
C=C^{\prime}\quad E=E^{\prime}\quad a^{\prime}=a^{\prime\prime}\quad K^{\prime}=K\cup\{s^{\prime}\}

Then, we can conclude:

(290)

\displaystyle\forall a\in t:\exists T^{a}_{m},T^{a}_{a},T^{a}_{p}:a\in\text{ServerRef}[T^{a}_{m},T^{a}_{a},T^{a}_{p}]\qquad

(291)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\

\displaystyle\forall a\in t^{\prime}:\exists T^{a}_{m},T^{a}_{a},T^{a}_{p}:a\in\text{ServerRef}[T^{a}_{m},T^{a}_{a},T^{a}_{p}]\qquad

\displaystyle(\text{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{conn-1:server-prog}})

(292)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\

\displaystyle\forall a\in t^{\prime}:a\in\text{ServerRef}[\llbracket C.\mathit{tm}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.\mathit{ta}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.\mathit{ap}\rrbracket]\qquad

\displaystyle(\text{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{encoding:server-prog}})

(293)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\

\displaystyle\forall a\in t^{\prime}:a=a^{\prime\prime}\wedge a\in\text{ServerRef}[\llbracket C^{\prime}.\mathit{tm}\rrbracket,\llbracket C^{\prime}.\mathit{ta}\rrbracket,\llbracket C^{\prime}.\mathit{ap}\rrbracket]\qquad

\displaystyle(\text{by {Sv-Connect}})

We can also conclude:

(294)

\displaystyle\forall s\in t^{\prime}:(s\in t\vee s=s^{\prime})\qquad

(295)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\

\displaystyle\forall s\in t^{\prime}:(s\in K\vee s=s^{\prime})\qquad

by the theorem premise

(296)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\

\displaystyle\forall s\in t^{\prime}:s\in K^{\prime}\qquad

\displaystyle\text{by the definition of }K^{\prime}

(297)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\

\displaystyle\forall s\in t^{\prime}:s\in K^{\prime}\wedge s\in\text{Chan}[\llbracket C.\mathit{tm}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.\mathit{ta}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.\mathit{ap}\rrbracket]\qquad

by
Sv-Connect

(298)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\

\displaystyle\forall s\in t^{\prime}:s\in K^{\prime}\wedge s\in\text{Chan}[\llbracket C^{\prime}.\mathit{tm}\rrbracket,\llbracket C^{\prime}.\mathit{ta}\rrbracket,\llbracket C^{\prime}.\mathit{ap}\rrbracket]\qquad

\displaystyle\text{by the definition of }C^{\prime}

•

\alpha="\text{read}(s^{\prime},v)=r"

where

(300)

\displaystyle\emptyset\vdash v:\text{P4Entity}\ T^{s^{\prime}}_{m}\ T^{s^{\prime}}_{a}\ T^{s^{\prime}}_{p}\ X_{n}\ X_{a}

(301)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle s^{\prime}\in t

(302)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\forall a:a\in t\Leftrightarrow a\in t^{\prime}

(303)

\displaystyle\wedge\

\displaystyle\forall s\in t^{\prime}:s\in t

The only rule that can reduce the server with label
\overline{\text{read}(s^{\prime},v)=r}
is
Sv-Read
:

(304)

\genfrac{}{}{0.5pt}{0}{\begin{gathered}s' \in K \quad\mathit{Conforms}(v,C) \quad\serverRedInt{C}{E}{\tx{read}(v)}{v'}\end{gathered}}{\serverRed{C, E, a', K}{\tx{read}(s',v)}{v'}{C, E, a', K}}

such that
S=S^{\prime}

From
eq.

301
and the premises of the theorem, we have
s^{\prime}\in K
.

Then, we also have that
s\in\text{Chan}[\llbracket C.\mathit{tm}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.\mathit{ta}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.\mathit{ap}\rrbracket]
,
which means that

\emptyset\vdash v:\text{P4Entity}\ \llbracket C.\mathit{tm}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.\mathit{ta}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.\mathit{ap}\rrbracket\ X_{n}\ X_{a}
.
From
definition

5.1
, we have

v\in\text{P4Entity}\ \llbracket C.\mathit{tm}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.\mathit{ta}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.\mathit{ap}\rrbracket\ X_{n}\ X_{a}
,
which is the same as
\mathit{Conforms}(v,C)
.

\langle C,E,\text{read}(v)\rangle\downarrow v^{\prime}
always yields a well-typed response,
so this premise always holds.

Then, we can conclude:

(305)

\displaystyle\forall a\in t:\exists T^{a}_{m},T^{a}_{a},T^{a}_{p}:a\in\text{ServerRef}[T^{a}_{m},T^{a}_{a},T^{a}_{p}]\qquad

(306)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\

\displaystyle\forall a\in t^{\prime}:\exists T^{a}_{m},T^{a}_{a},T^{a}_{p}:a\in\text{ServerRef}[T^{a}_{m},T^{a}_{a},T^{a}_{p}]\qquad

\displaystyle(\text{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{read-2:server-prog}})

(307)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\

\displaystyle\forall a\in t^{\prime}:a\in\text{ServerRef}[\llbracket C.m_{m}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.m_{a}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.m_{p}\rrbracket]\qquad

\displaystyle(\text{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{encoding:server-prog}})

(308)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\

\displaystyle\forall a\in t^{\prime}:a=a^{\prime\prime}\wedge a\in\text{ServerRef}[\llbracket C^{\prime}.m_{m}\rrbracket,\llbracket C^{\prime}.m_{a}\rrbracket,\llbracket C^{\prime}.m_{p}\rrbracket]\qquad

\displaystyle(\text{by {Sv-Read}})

We can also conclude:

(309)

\displaystyle\forall s\in t^{\prime}:s\in t\qquad

(310)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\

\displaystyle\forall s\in t^{\prime}:s\in K\wedge s\in\text{Chan}[,\llbracket C.m_{m}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.m_{a}\rrbracket,\llbracket C.m_{p}\rrbracket]\qquad

by the theorem premise

(311)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\

\displaystyle\forall s\in t^{\prime}:s\in K^{\prime}\wedge s\in\text{Chan}[\llbracket C^{\prime}.m_{m}\rrbracket,\llbracket C^{\prime}.m_{a}\rrbracket,\llbracket C^{\prime}.m_{p}\rrbracket]\qquad

\displaystyle\text{by the definition of }S^{\prime}

•

\alpha="\text{insert}(s^{\prime},v)=b"

Analogous to the case for
\text{read}(s^{\prime},v)=r
, only using the
Sv-Insert
rule.

•

\alpha="\text{modify}(s^{\prime},v)=b"

Analogous to the case for
\text{read}(s^{\prime},v)=r
, only using the
Sv-Modify
rule.

•

\alpha="\text{delete}(s^{\prime},v)=b"

Analogous to the case for
\text{read}(s^{\prime},v)=r
, only using the
Sv-Delete
rule.

See
6.4

Proof.

Direct consequence of
Theorem

B.19
(which embeds in its
statements the conditions of
Definition

6.3
),

Proposition

B.15
and
Definition

6.2
.
